- **Paid by** — the trust, estate account, law firm, or the client. In estate work the payer is frequently *not* the client, and this is what tells you the estate is funding the engagement properly. It also settles the question if heirs later dispute who paid for what.
- **Photo of the cheque** — filed to Drive and linked to the payment. Five seconds, and you want it before the cheque leaves your hands. This is what makes a hand-recorded payment a document rather than an assertion.

**Partial payments are normal and handled.** Two cheques, or a trust sending part and the family the rest, all work: the job stays *part-paid* and unfunded until the running total reaches the target, the *Deposit received* row reads *Part paid — $X of $Y*, and **✓ Record payment** stays on it for the next one. A short total asks before it's accepted; a second cheque that completes the deposit passes without a warning.

> **Work starts on *received*, not *cleared*.** Waiting for cheques to clear costs three to five days on every job. Wire transfers and cash are recorded as cleared on receipt. A cheque, or a bank transfer you record by hand, is recorded as *uncleared*: the confirmation says so, and the recorder's list of payments at that stage marks it. It stays uncleared until the bank confirms it and somebody presses **✓ Mark cleared** beside it on the dashboard's payments list (below; this note said until 2026-09-30 that nothing in the app cleared it). A bank transfer Stripe reports is recorded as cleared, because the app records it only once it has settled, and one you recorded by hand is cleared when Stripe confirms it (§8b). At or above **$10,000** a personal cheque is no longer the accepted instrument: **wire preferred, cashier's cheque accepted**. Recording a large personal cheque anyway is allowed, since the money is already in hand. The recorder warns you, and the payment is stored as a *policy exception*. Like every hand-recorded payment it is attributed to the manager who approved this job's price, because the app has no sign-in to say who pressed the button. (This note used to say uncleared payments showed on the dashboard until the money settled, and that the exception carried your name.)

A *certified* cheque and a *cashier's* cheque are not the same thing: certified is drawn on the client's account with the bank only secondarily liable; a cashier's cheque is drawn on the bank's own account with the money already taken. Ask for the second one. Neither is bounce-proof — counterfeit cashier's cheques are a common fraud — so on a large deposit, phone the *issuing* bank on a number you look up yourself, not the one printed on the cheque.

### The payments list: Mark cleared and Void

**New 2026-09-30.** Every payment recorded on a client is listed under the timeline on the Client Dashboard, headed *Payments recorded*: the amount, the stage, the method, the day it was received, who paid it, and its state — *Uncleared*; *Cleared* with the day and who (or *Stripe reported it settled*); or *Void* with the day, who and why. A deposit recorded before payment records existed has no method and reads *amount inferred — predates payment records*: its amount is the 50% target, not a figure anybody counted, as the recorder also says (its *Already recorded at this stage* list reads it the same way since 2026-10-06, P22, where it printed *· —* for the method). The list is there on every state of the job, closed and retained included. Until then no screen listed the payments, nothing cleared a cheque, and nothing took back a payment recorded in error.

- **✓ Mark cleared** — on a payment that counts and is uncleared. Press it once the bank shows the money as final. It asks first, then records today and the manager who approved this job's price, as every payment is attributed (the app has no sign-in). It moves no money: the job was funded on the money received.
- **Void** — on any payment that still counts: a cheque that came back unpaid, a payment typed twice, one recorded on the wrong client. The dialog names the payment and **says what the void does to the money before anything is written**: what comes off the stage, whether the job stops being funded (and so whether hours can still be logged), whether the timeline will show *Deposit voided: record the replacement payment* (below), whether the final reads unpaid again, the amount a closed job records as retained, and the job's received total. A reason is required. **The payment is not deleted**: it stays on the list, struck through, marked void with its reason, and counts toward nothing — not the deposit, not the invoices' received rows or balance, not the recorder's prefill, not the retained amount. Then record the money the way it really arrived.

> **A void is for good, and a recorded payment is never edited — decided with Anthony, 2026-10-01.** There is no un-void and no way to change a payment's amount, date or method once it is recorded: to correct one, void it with the reason and record it again. Removal is a recorded act, so the wrong entry stays on the list, struck through, beside the right one. On a current deployment (Apps Script `2026-09-30b`, §2) a void and a clear also hold against a device that had not reloaded and then changed the same payment.

> **A deposit voided on a job that had been paid for: a red step, and nothing rewinds (since 2026-10-01).** When the void takes the deposit below its target — the cheque that funded the job came back, on a job that may already be *Active* — the timeline's *Deposit received* step is **blocked**: the band turns red and reads ***Deposit voided: record the replacement payment***, names the payment, the day it was voided and why, and prints the fix under it (press **✓ Record payment** when the replacement arrives). The job **stays active**, *Job active* keeps its day and its name, and nothing else is undone; hours cannot be logged until a payment that counts restores the deposit, and then the step goes green and the band moves on. It is read from the payments alone, never the job's status: a void that leaves the deposit whole (a payment typed twice) raises nothing, and a deposit that was never whole reads *Part paid* as before. (Until 2026-10-01 the band simply went back to *Collect the deposit*.)

**Refunds are on the list too (since 2026-10-01).** A refund recorded on an hourly walkaway (*Closing out a job that dies*, §9) is listed as money that went back — *Refund*, its method, reference and payee, and *Refund sent* with the day and who recorded it — and the heading reads *Payments and refunds recorded*. It counts toward nothing a payment counts toward. It has no **Mark cleared**; one recorded in error is **voided** with a reason, exactly as a payment is, and the dialog says first what the retained amount and the refund due become.

### Payment Schedule

| Milestone | Amount | Timing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Deposit | 50% of estimate | Due upon signing (estimate basis — no actuals yet) |
| Midpoint | Brings cumulative to 75%, with the home-prep fee trued to actual Job Plan quotes | Due at project midpoint (Home Prep: the second payment, once the vendor schedule is booked) |
| Final | Balance — hourly: labor trued to logged hours, a Change Order's hours included; fixed price: the rest of the fee plus each accepted Change Order's price; fees on actuals | Within 7 days of final invoice |

Third-party and home-prep vendor invoices are billed directly to the client at cost. Havellin's 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee on home-prep vendors — on a standalone engagement, on prep bundled into a labor job (since 2026-09-10) *and* on a fixed-price job, where it is its own line on top of the flat fee (since 2026-09-23) — is calculated on the actual quotes logged in the Job Plan sourcing, including a prep vendor an accepted change order added (since 2026-09-30, §9), and trued up from the midpoint invoice onward; no fee is charged on any other third-party vendor; lines with no logged quote fall back to the estimate, tagged "est." Each stage is generated from its own row on the job timeline (§9a).

> **⚠⚠ BOTH FORMS NOW COVER THE WRITTEN RECORD, AND UNTIL 2026-09-21 NEITHER DID.** Every custody, retention and non-disclosure promise in the two agreements attached to a defined term, *Documentation Media*, which means **photographs and video**. Once the item record went to all six labour services (§10a), both engagements began producing a **written** document naming who took what — a daughter, a charity, an auction house — held for seven years, and none of those promises reached it. The standard form’s general confidentiality clause (§9) is looser again: *commercially reasonable efforts*, with disclosure permitted to any vendor who *needs it*, which is not a standard to hold a list of third-party names to. Both forms now define **Project Records** and put them on the same terms as the images: same folder, same named-access rule, same seven years, never sold or licensed, and **expressly outside the marketing clause** — a client who does not tick the opt-out is not thereby authorising publication of a list naming their daughter.

|  | Standard form (living client) | Estate / probate form |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Where it sits | New **§10.1b**. §10 is renamed *Documentation and Records Authorization* — it is no longer only about photography | Appended to **§7.1**, renamed *Documentation Media and Project Records*. **No new number**, so Termination, Dispute Resolution and General Provisions do not shift against agreements already issued citing them |
| Who may see it | The Client, and anyone they authorise in writing | The PR, counsel of record, anyone the PR authorises — and **produced as required by law or court order** |
| Naming a recipient | Stated as the Client’s own instruction, used for nothing else, and the Client may ask in writing that a name be left out | No such opt-out — a PR cannot suppress a beneficiary’s name from a fiduciary record. Cites the **signed receipt §5.3 already requires** |
| What it disclaims | Not an appraisal, not evidence of title, not a tax / insurance / estate-planning record | The same, plus: **not** the Fla. Stat. §733.604 inventory, and no evidence that a distribution satisfied a devise or bequest |

> **Three asymmetries are deliberate; do not “harmonise” the two forms.** The estate form has a production duty because somebody else is entitled to the record; the living form does not, because a client’s record is theirs. Only the living form lets a recipient go unnamed. And only the estate form **carves the record out of §7’s return-or-destroy bullet** — *“Destroy or return all Client documents upon project completion or written request”* sits five bullets above a seven-year fiduciary retention. It reads as covering the estate’s *own* documents we encounter (the will, deeds, financial records) rather than records we create, but a contract is construed against its drafter and that is not a thing to leave to inference on a matter a court may read.

> **⚠ NOT REVIEWED, AND THREE THINGS GO TO COUNSEL WITH IT.** This language was drafted here, like the rest of both forms, and Anthony is having the whole of both reviewed before the first real engagement signs. Flag these three specifically. **(1)** The standard form now states two confidentiality standards — §9’s *any vendor who needs it* against §10.1a/§10.1b’s *a vendor performing Services on this engagement*; the narrower should govern and §9 may want tightening rather than relying on the specific overriding the general. **(2)** Seven years was chosen to match the images and is not derived from anything; a written record naming non-parties may warrant a different period. **(3)** Those non-parties never signed anything, and their names sit in our hands for seven years — worth sizing against the insurance position.

## 8a. Sending an agreement through DocuSign

**Live since 2026-09-17.** An agreement can go out two ways, and **the choice is made per job, on the job, not in Settings**. Anthony's reason: *"if somebody is old school and we need to just send them a PDF to sign, we can do that — but we don't need to go into the app and change the settings overall."*

| | DocuSign (the default, firm-wide) | Send as a PDF |
|---|---|---|
| **What you press** | **✉ Send for signature — DocuSign** on the *Signing packet sent* row. **The button names the route it will take**, so the two are never confused at the moment of pressing. | **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand**, beside it. |
| **Who sends it** | DocuSign emails the client directly, and on an estate each co-representative recorded beside them (since 2026-10-05). | You do — a Gmail draft with the packet attached, which you read and send yourself. |
| **✓ I've sent it** | **Not shown.** There is nothing to confirm. | Shown. Press it once the email has gone. |
| **The signature** | Arrives **on its own**. The row reads *DocuSign is watching for it*, over **↻ Check DocuSign now**. | **✓ Record the signed agreement**, by hand (§8). |
| **Who is named as signer** | The client, found by the recipient id DocuSign returns for them (recipient 1; since 2026-10-05 never as *the first signer*, because each co-representative signs at the client's routing order too), on the day they signed (since 2026-09-30); and each co-representative, on their own day. (This row used to say the first signer, on the day the envelope completed.) | Whoever you type in, on the date they signed; each co-representative by the page they signed, filed from the timeline (§8). |
| **The executed copy** | Filed to the client's **Agreement** folder automatically, with its **certificate of completion**. | Yours to file. |

**Co-representatives sign in the same envelope (since 2026-10-05, P20; Anthony's answer to Q22).** On an estate, every co-executor or co-trustee recorded on the client signs in DocuSign beside the client: each is a signer at the client's routing order, 1, with a signature and a date tab on their own block of the signature page and a recipientId of their own (the client keeps 1, Anthony 2, agreements@ 3, the co-representatives 4 onward), and Anthony countersigns once all of them have. The send's notice names everyone it went to (*Sent to Ruth Adler and Daniel Adler for signature through DocuSign*), and while it is out the *Agreement signed* row says *Daniel Adler signs there too*. **DocuSign needs each one's email**, so a co-representative with none stops the send before anything is posted, naming everyone missing at once: *"Daniel Adler has no email recorded. Add it on Edit Client, or send the PDF to sign by hand."* On the PDF route they sign the printed page and you file it (§8). On an Apps Script deployment older than 2026-10-05 (§2) the envelope goes to the client and Anthony alone: the notice says the co-representatives were not put on it, and the row says they sign a printed copy. **Two signers at one email address (since 2026-10-05, P21; Q32).** A co-representative recorded with the client's address, or another's (a couple sharing an inbox, or a slip on Edit Client), still goes on the envelope, and the send's notice turns amber and names it: *"Ruth Adler and Daniel Adler are on the envelope at one email address (ruth@adler.example): make sure each of them signs for themselves."* It is read off who the backend says it put on the envelope, so an older deployment's send (the co-representatives left off) says nothing of it, and the sign-by-hand route never does. How DocuSign treats two signers at one address is not measured yet: Anthony tries it in the sandbox, and if DocuSign refuses it the app will refuse the send by name instead.

**A third way, since 2026-10-01: the packet handed over in person.** **🤝 Handed over in person** sits beside both buttons on every unsent agreement, for a printed packet given to the client across a table. It records the send itself (nothing is emailed, and nothing goes to DocuSign), the row reads *Handed over in person*, and the signature is recorded by hand, as on the PDF route (§8).

> **⚠⚠ THE ROUTE IS DECIDED WHEN YOU SEND, AND IT IS FIXED FROM THAT MOMENT.** Both buttons are offered side by side until one of them is pressed; afterwards **only the route you chose is offered**. That is not tidiness: an agreement already sitting in somebody's DocuSign inbox must not also be recorded as signed by hand, or the app and DocuSign disagree about whether a contract exists — and a paper job must never be waited on by a watcher that has no envelope. **A job papered before DocuSign was switched on keeps its manual button**, permanently; turning DocuSign on does not strand work already in flight.

> **⚠ You cannot mark a DocuSign agreement signed by hand, and that is the point.** The manual recorder is withdrawn and refuses to open on a job with a live envelope. Without that, somebody ticks it while DocuSign still says *sent* and the two records disagree about a contract. **If the client says they signed and the app has not caught up, open the client** — that is what triggers the check.

### What the client actually fills in

**Two things, plus one optional box**: their signature, the date, and a marketing opt-out they may leave alone. **The box is on both forms** — living-client and estate. Nothing else is a field: no initials, no role tick box, no second signature.

| Field | Required? | Where it is |
|---|---|---|
| Client signature and date | **Yes** | The signature block at the end, which is now a page of its own. |
| Each co-representative's signature and date (an estate, since 2026-10-05) | **Yes**, from each of them | Their own *Co-Signer* block on the same page; the client never signs for them. |
| *I DO NOT AUTHORIZE* marketing use | **No — leaving it blank authorizes** | §10.2 on the living-client form, §7.2 on the estate one, at the end of the section. **Both forms.** The client's box alone: a co-representative signing beside them has none, and opts out in writing (§7.2; Anthony, 2026-10-05, Q29). |

> **⚠⚠ IT IS AN OPT-OUT AND SILENCE MEANS YES. Say that plainly if a client asks.** §10.2 authorizes marketing use by default; the box at the end of it is how they decline, and leaving it alone is what authorizes. Ticking it changes nothing about the work, the fee, or any other term, and the section says so on its face. **They can also decline, or withdraw an authorization already given, at any time in writing** — the box is a convenience, not the only way, and that sentence is in §10.2 as well.
>
> **⚠⚠ THIS REVERSED A BUILD THAT WAS ONE DAY OLD, AND THE REASON IS WORTH KNOWING BEFORE ANYBODY PROPOSES CHANGING IT BACK.** For a few hours on 2026-09-18 it was an opt-in: a required *I AUTHORIZE* / *I DO NOT AUTHORIZE* pair with a signature that appeared only under *authorize*. The reasoning was sound — DocuSign's guided navigation jumps past optional fields, so an optional consent is one nobody is ever asked for. What went wrong was not the reasoning but the fields: **on the first envelope DocuSign really sent, the radio buttons and the conditional signature were simply not on the page.** Anthony: *"docusign did not recognize the marketing tic boxes or the required signature. why don't we just simplify that as one 'I do not authorize' … allow them to opt out, but use for marketing is assumed unless tic'ed."* A single checkbox is the most basic field DocuSign has.
>
> **⚠ AND THE OPT-OUT FAILS SAFE IN A WAY THE OPT-IN COULD NOT.** Under the opt-in, a field that silently failed to place left a required question unasked and the record blank. Under the opt-out, a box nobody sees lands on the documented default — authorized — which is exactly what the clause says happens when nothing is ticked, and the client's written-notice route survives either way.

> **⚠⚠ THE ESTATE FORM ASKS THE SAME QUESTION, AND THE PROHIBITION IT REPLACES LASTED ONE DAY.** For a few hours on 2026-09-18, §7.2 read *Marketing & Promotional Use — Not Permitted* and offered nothing to tick, on the reasoning that a personal representative consenting to publish a decedent's house is a fiduciary trading something that is not theirs. Anthony pushed back the same evening and was right: *"what if we get an estate clean out and they don't care? … estate sale companies take pictures at estate sales … as long as we're not disclosing the client's name or their address or showing a living room with like pictures of their family in it so people can identify them, maybe it's fine. And all the documents should just provide the opt-out."* Three things settled it:
>
> - **The anonymity standard he described was already the clause.** (a)–(e) forbid the street address, the names, images of identifiable people, anything bearing identifying information, and media that would clearly identify a residence. The question was never whether we can anonymise.
> - **A personal representative may SELL the contents outright** under §733.607. Authorising a photograph is the smaller act, not the larger one.
> - **Estate sale companies publish room-by-room photographs with the address attached**, and families sign that every week. It is industry-normal.
>
> **So it is one clause and one box on both forms.** Two contracts saying different things about the same photographs is how the rule governing a client's own home comes to depend on which form they happened to sign.

> **⚠⚠ TWO RULES SURVIVE THE REVERSAL AND NEITHER IS IN THE CONTRACT — they are ours to follow, and nothing in the app enforces them.**
>
> - **Do not publish until the house has closed or been cleared.** The risk is not legal, it is that an empty Palm Beach estate whose contents were just posted is a targeting problem a living-client job does not have. Estate sale companies accept that because getting buyers to the address on Saturday is the entire point; we would be accepting it for nothing.
> - **Never publish a contested matter at all.** A representative's discretionary acts are exactly what gets challenged, and *"you put our mother's house on Instagram"* is free ammunition even where it is perfectly lawful. The signed box does not help you here.
>
> Neither is a contract clause, deliberately. Both are judgement calls made after the document is signed, and writing them into one form and not the other is exactly the drift the shared clause exists to prevent.

> **⚠ AND NEITHER FORM MAY CONTRADICT ITS OWN OPT-OUT.** Both did, briefly, and both are corrected. The custody clause (§10.1a / §7.1) said Documentation Media *"is never sold, licensed, or shared with any third party"* — an absolute, a few paragraphs above a section authorising publication. The estate form's §7 confidentiality list separately forbade posting *"without express written consent"*, which is an opt-IN sitting over an opt-out. **A contract stating both is construed against whoever drafted it**, so the practical effect would have been that we operated under the stricter rule while believing we had the looser one. Each now names the marketing section as its one stated exception.

> **Documentation photography is not a choice and is not asked about.** We photograph the property and its contents because that is how the inventory, the appraisal support and the chain of custody are built — a client paying us to inventory their home is not separately consenting to the inventory. It used to carry an *initials* box, which was removed on 2026-09-18: *"if they are paying us to inventory their home, we're obviously going to inventory their home. Let's just state what we do and how we treat that confidential information."* The clause now says plainly where the media is stored, who can see it, that it is never sold or licensed, and that it is not published except as the marketing section permits (§10.1a standard, §7.1 estate).
>
> **⚠ The estate/probate form had no photography or marketing section at all until 2026-09-18**, and it is the form on the matters where the media is most sensitive. It carries **§7.1 Documentation Media** now — where the images live, who can reach them, the seven-year retention, never sold or licensed — and **§7.2 carrying the same marketing clause and the same opt-out box as the living-client form**. Both forms say what happens to the media, and both offer the same choice.

> **⚠⚠ §7 NO LONGER PROMISES A PER-JOB NDA, BECAUSE HAVELLIN DOES NOT TAKE ONE (corrected 2026-09-20).** The estate and probate form's *Confidentiality & Privacy* list opened by binding Havellin to *"require all team members and contractors to sign a Non-Disclosure Agreement before accessing the property"* — a written undertaking to a personal representative, on the matters where the contents are most sensitive, that the firm does not perform. Anthony: *"we do not require NDAs for staff on all jobs. confidentiality is baked into our 1099 employment agreements."* The bullet states the real mechanism now: every team member and contractor is **engaged under a written agreement carrying confidentiality obligations, executed before they work on any Havellin engagement**.
>
> **The duty is unchanged in substance, and standing is stronger than per-job** — nobody works a Havellin engagement at all without it, so there is no job on which it was not already in force. What went is a promise about *paperwork collected on the day*, which is the part nothing performed.
>
> **⚠ The living-client form is untouched** — its §9 never made the NDA promise, so there was nothing to correct there. The **Job Plan lost its matching box the same day** (§11). **An agreement issued before 2026-09-20 needs no re-issuing:** what the crew is actually bound by is broader than the clause described. ⚠ Do not restore either half without the process behind it.

> **Nothing asks the signer to classify themselves any more.** Who has authority to sign is established at intake and we are talking to that person, so a pick-one on the contract was a question with no reader.
>
> **⚠ THE ESTATE FORM STILL STATES THE ROLE, AND THAT IS NOT THE SAME THING.** *Role / Authority* at §1.2 and *Title / Role* on its signature page are the **capacity that lets that person bind the estate** — exactly what *Managing Member* does on our side of the block, and what counsel queries on a court-reviewed matter. They print **the role recorded at intake**. What came off is the **four-option menu** they used to fall back to (*Personal Representative · Executor · POA · Other*), which on an executed contract — and on the signature page, beside the line the representative signs — reads as a pick-one they are meant to answer. No e-signature field was ever placed on it.

### What the document itself looks like — three changes off a real envelope

Anthony read the first agreement DocuSign actually sent and flagged three things on its face. All three are fixed, and an agreement generated before 2026-09-18 will not match what is described here — regenerate it rather than reconciling it.

| What went | Why |
|---|---|
| The boxed banner at the top — *"IMPORTANT: Read carefully. Both parties must sign before any work begins."* | *"the top banner is not needed and sounds amateurish."* Both halves of it are said better further down: the estate form's signature page states that no work begins until the Client and Havellin have signed (since 2026-10-05; it read *"until both signatures are obtained"*), and the acknowledgment clause carries the read-and-understood line. |
| The *Exhibit A — Service Estimate* paragraph at the very end of the standard form | *"the second is duplicated on the actual exhibit header, so unneeded."* The agreement never goes out alone — it is always the signing packet — and the packet already opens the estimate under its own *Exhibit A* band. **The incorporation is still stated in §1.1**, which is where a contract states it. |
| The signature block sharing a page with the last clause | *"should we have a page break at the end of the agreement so the signature page is always it's own page?"* It was real: measured on the envelope that went out, the standard form put our block on page 6 and the client's on page 7, and the estate form split 7/8. Both now land whole on one page. |

> **The signature page is a page of its own on both forms, on paper and in DocuSign alike.** Measured after the change: the standard form is 7 pages with the whole signature block on page 7, and the estate form is **7 pages rather than 8**.

> **⚠ The reason given for the estate form's saved sheet was that the marketing consent block had been dropped from it, and that block came back the same evening** when §7.2 became the shared opt-out. Re-measured before and after that change with one method on both sides: **restoring the clause added no sheet to either form** — the block is one short paragraph run plus a tick box, and it lands inside space the page already had.
>
> **⚠ The rule lives in the app's main stylesheet, not just in the print block, and that is what makes it reach DocuSign.** The PDF the envelope carries is built by inlining every stylesheet in the page; a rule that only existed under `@media print` would paginate the Print button and do nothing to the document the client signs.

### How the signature gets back

**There is no timer and nothing runs in the background.** The app asks DocuSign when somebody is looking: on a page load, and when you open a client. So **opening the client is the refresh** — if you are waiting on a signature, open the job.

- **It asks at most once every 20 minutes per agreement.** DocuSign's published limit is one request per document per 15 minutes and **the penalty is losing API access altogether**, not a warning. Opening the same client six times in a row asks once.
- **↻ Check DocuSign now is the one way to make it look, and it obeys the same 20 minutes.** It sits on the *Agreement signed* row while an envelope is out. Press it inside the window and it does not ask — it tells you **the time it can ask again** and says nothing is lost by waiting. Pressing it ten times sends nothing. **It never writes a signature itself**; it asks DocuSign and records only what DocuSign says, which is the same rule that withdraws the manual button.
- **Only *completed* counts as signed.** DocuSign reports *delivered* when the client opens the email and *signed* when one recipient is done — the agreement has two signers (the client, then Havellin), and on an estate one more for each co-representative, beside the client, so neither of those is an executed contract and neither marks the job signed.
- **A failed check says so, and says what it does not mean.** If DocuSign cannot be reached the app tells you the reason and states plainly *"It may already be signed; this is not a statement that it is not."* A silent failure would leave a signed agreement reading unsigned forever.
- **A signed agreement stops being checked.**

> **⚠ THE CERTIFICATE OF COMPLETION IS FETCHED DELIBERATELY AND IT MATTERS ON PROBATE.** Two files land in the **Agreement** folder: the executed agreement (named *… - SIGNED*) and the **certificate of completion**, which is DocuSign's audit trail of who signed, from what address, and when. On a court-reviewed matter that is the evidence of *who bound the estate*. DocuSign does not include it unless it is asked for, and **if it cannot be retrieved the app says so in amber rather than filing the agreement quietly**.
>
> It is fetched when the app first records the signature. **Re-opening the client does not try again**: once the signature is recorded the job leaves the check. **If either file does not land, the *Agreement signed* row offers 📁 File signed copy (since 2026-10-01; Anthony)**, in the strip under the timeline, for as long as the executed agreement or the certificate is missing from Drive, and gone once both are there. One press fetches both from DocuSign and files them, **replacing** a copy already there rather than adding a second (Apps Script `2026-10-01`, §2), and records what it filed. A document download counts against DocuSign's limit like a status check, so a press within 20 minutes of the last request is refused, naming the time it can ask again. The failure notices say to press it. (Until 2026-09-30 this note said re-opening the client retried the fetch; until 2026-10-01 it, and the notices, said to download both from DocuSign by hand.)
>
> **Once they are filed, the row offers both (since 2026-10-06, P22):** **📁 Filed signed packet**, the executed agreement, and **📁 Filed certificate of completion**, each opening its copy in Drive. The links were recorded and offered nowhere until then. **A fetch that has had no answer after two minutes lets go** and says so (*It may still have been filed: press File signed copy on the timeline to try again*); until then a request that never answered left File signed copy refusing every press as already under way until the page was reloaded.

> **Havellin countersigns second, and the envelope is not complete until we do.** The client signs first (routing order 1, with each co-representative beside them on an estate since 2026-10-05), then the agreement comes to Havellin. DocuSign only reports *completed* when every signer is done, so the app cannot mark a job signed off a half-executed contract. **An envelope sitting at *signed* is usually waiting on us** — check the DocuSign inbox.
>
> **⚠ THE COUNTERSIGNATURE GOES TO ANTHONY PERSONALLY, NOT TO `agreements@`, AND THE REASON IS THE AUDIT TRAIL.** A department address is a Google Group: whoever opened it first would sign, and the certificate of completion would record that signature under a name that may not be the person who clicked. On a contract, the audit trail naming the wrong human is the one failure it exists to prevent. **`agreements@` is copied instead**, at routing order 3 — so the firm gets the executed agreement on file *after* every signature, never an unsigned one that reads in the inbox like an executed one. The client and Anthony both receive the completed copy from DocuSign as well.

> **⚠⚠ FIXED 2026-09-18 — AN AGREEMENT SENT THROUGH DOCUSIGN BEFORE THIS DATE GOT STUCK, AND IT REPAIRS ITSELF.** Reported live, on an envelope both parties had already signed: the job sat on *Signing packet sent* and never moved on to the deposit. The send really did go out and the signature really did come back — **nothing was ever lost, and nothing in DocuSign was wrong**. The app recorded the send on the document but not on the job, and three things then failed in silence: it stopped watching the envelope, so it never asked DocuSign for the status; it would have refused the signature if it had; and nothing on screen said so.
>
> **What to do: reload the app, then open the client.** That is the whole repair — the app now reads the document record rather than the flag, so a stuck job corrects on sight, asks DocuSign, records the signature, and files the executed copy and the certificate of completion to Drive as it should have done at the time. There is nothing to re-send and nothing to re-sign.
>
> **⚠ One thing to check on a job that was stuck:** while it was stuck the band went on offering *Send signing packet*. If anybody pressed it a second time, **look in DocuSign for a duplicate envelope on that client** and void the one nobody signed. One agreement must not have two envelopes against it.

### Switching it on

**There is nothing to switch on in Settings, and the control that used to be there was removed on 2026-09-18.** DocuSign is the firm's route for every agreement, on every device. *It used to be a per-device dropdown defaulting to "Recorded by hand"*, which meant a concierge who had never opened Settings sent agreements the old way without knowing there was another — and that is exactly what happened: an agreement went out as a plain email on one browser while another was on DocuSign. **How a firm signs is not a per-browser preference.** The per-job choice is unaffected: **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand** is still on every unsent agreement, and so, since 2026-10-01, is **🤝 Handed over in person**.

Five values must be set in the Apps Script project's **Script Properties**, where the Quo key lives:

| Property | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| `DS_INTEGRATION_KEY` | DocuSign → Settings → Apps and Keys → the app's *Integration Key*. |
| `DS_USER_ID` | The same page — *User ID* (a GUID), **not** the email address. |
| `DS_ACCOUNT_ID` | *API Account ID*. |
| `DS_PRIVATE_KEY` | The RSA private key generated beside the integration key. |
| `DS_BASE_URI` | `https://demo.docusign.net` while testing, the account's live base URI in production. **Everything else follows from it** — the app never has a second switch that could disagree about which environment it is talking to. |

> **⚠⚠ THE PRIVATE KEY GOES INTO SCRIPT PROPERTIES AND NOWHERE ELSE. Never paste it into a chat, a document, a terminal, or an online converter.** This repository is public and the app is served from GitHub Pages, so a key in either is not a key. A Google client secret was pasted into a thread on 2026-09-08 and had to be reset.
>
> **Paste the key exactly as DocuSign gives it**, including the `-----BEGIN` and `-----END` lines. **DocuSign issues a format Apps Script cannot use, and the script converts it itself** — there is no command to run and nothing to install. An earlier build printed an `openssl` command instead; within minutes of following it the key had been executed line by line as shell commands and left in the terminal history. That is why the app does the conversion.

> **Run `testEsignAuth` from the Apps Script editor before the first envelope.** It proves the five properties, the key and the account in one call and names which one is wrong. **The first run will ask for consent** — it prints a URL to open once, in the browser signed in as the DocuSign user; nothing works until that is granted, and it is granted once per account. **Two gates, one error each, and the second is invisible until the first clears**: a key problem and a consent problem look nothing alike, so read the error rather than guessing.

> **⚠ THE ONE THING TO CHECK ON A REAL ENVELOPE, BEFORE TRUSTING IT ON A CLIENT.** DocuSign finds the signature boxes by searching the PDF for invisible markers in the signature block. That the markers are *in* the document is proven; **where DocuSign draws the box relative to them can only be seen**. Send one envelope to yourself in the demo account and look at it before the first client gets one. **Done: Anthony has sent envelopes through it that worked (reported 2026-09-30).** It is worth doing again after any change to the signature page.

## 8b. Taking the deposit by bank transfer (ACH)

**Built 2026-09-18.** The deposit row of the client's timeline carries **🏦 ACH payment link** beside **✉ Send deposit invoice** — and, once that invoice has gone out, beside **✓ Record payment** until the deposit is in. Press it, and the app creates a Stripe payment link for exactly what is still outstanding on the deposit and shows you the URL. **Send it yourself, with the invoice** — the app never mails it, the same rule that makes every client email a draft you read before it goes.

> **⚠⚠ AFTER A PART CHEQUE THE LINK ASKS FOR THE BALANCE (2026-09-29, Anthony: agreed).** A client who has sent $2,000 against a $4,575 deposit gets a link for **$2,575**, and Stripe's own page reads *Deposit (50%) balance*, so the smaller figure does not read as a mistake. Until then the link asked for the whole deposit — a client who used it overpaid by the cheque — and it was offered only beside *Send deposit invoice*, so once the invoice had gone out there was no link to send at all.
>
> **⚠ A LINK MADE BEFORE A CHEQUE ARRIVED IS NOT HANDED BACK AS CURRENT.** One link per stage still holds, so pressing the button again brings back the link already made — and if it now asks for more than is owed, the app says so instead of showing it: *"This ACH link asks for $2,575, but only $1,575 is outstanding … Do not send it: the client would overpay by $1,000."* A link cannot be edited once made. **Deactivate it in the Stripe Dashboard under Payment links, and take the balance by cheque or wire.**

> **⚠⚠ BANK TRANSFER ONLY. CARDS ARE NOT ACCEPTED, AND THAT IS A PRICING DECISION RATHER THAN A SETTING.** On a $25,715 job a card costs Havellin **$746.63** and a bank transfer costs **$16.50**. A "3% convenience fee" cannot close that gap legally: the card networks cap a surcharge at the *lower* of 3% or what acceptance actually costs you, and Stripe's card price works out above 2.9% on every ticket — so 3% is only chargeable at or below about **$300**. Anthony, asked directly on 2026-09-18: *"No cards at all."*
>
> **⚠ AND THE APP ENFORCES IT RATHER THAN TRUSTING THE SETTING.** After creating a link it reads it back, and if Stripe would also take a card on it the link is **switched off and never shown to you**, with a message naming the fix (Stripe Dashboard → Settings → Payments → Payment methods: cards off, ACH Direct Debit on). A link you cannot see is a link no client can pay by card.

> **⚠⚠ IT RECORDS ITSELF, AND ONLY WHEN THE MONEY HAS GENUINELY SETTLED.** A bank transfer is not instant: the client authorises it, and about **four business days** later it actually arrives. Stripe reports those as two different things and **the app waits for the second one**. When it lands, the next check writes the payment: the amount, the payer's name or email, and, as the day it was received, the date the client authorised the transfer (Stripe's payment date, a few business days before the money arrived — this note used to call it the date the money moved). It is marked **cleared on the day the app saw it settle** (since 2026-09-30; it used to be marked cleared on the authorisation date, days before the money was in — Stripe's answer carries no settlement date, so the app records the day it learned of it, which is on or after it). It names **Stripe** as who recorded it, and funds the job once the deposit is complete. You do not type anything.
>
> **⚠ SO A CLIENT SAYING "I'VE PAID" AND THE APP SAYING NOTHING IS NORMAL FOR A FEW DAYS.** They have authorised it; the money has not arrived. Do not record it by hand to make the screen agree with them — that is the app asserting money is in the account when it is not, on the record of what the firm is owed.
>
> **⚠ If it was recorded by hand anyway, it is not recorded twice (since 2026-09-30).** When Stripe reports a transfer settled and the client already has a *Bank transfer (ACH)* recorded by hand at that stage for the same amount, with no Stripe reference of its own, that payment takes Stripe's reference and is marked cleared, and the notice says so. Until then Stripe's report was written beside it and every total counted the money twice. A deposit such a transfer funds stays watched until Stripe confirms it (a funded deposit used to drop off the check, leaving the transfer uncleared for good). Anything less exact — a different amount, another stage, a cheque — is recorded on its own, as two payments of one size are possible.

> **⚠ THE CHECK HAPPENS WHEN THE APP LOADS AND WHEN YOU OPEN THE CLIENT, and nothing runs on a timer**, so a payment that landed overnight appears the first time somebody opens the app or that job. If you are waiting on one, open the client. Opening it twice in ten minutes makes no second check. A link stops being checked once its invoice is paid: the deposit always did, and since 2026-10-06 (P22) the midpoint and final do too, once money has come in at that stage and its invoice has nothing outstanding (a transfer recorded by hand and still waiting for Stripe keeps it watched). (This note used to name opening the client as the only check; loading the app checks the outstanding links too.)

> **⚠ ONE LINK PER STAGE, AND PRESSING THE BUTTON AGAIN RETURNS THE SAME ONE.** Two links against one invoice is two ways to pay it, and a client who pays both has overpaid by a deposit. The amount comes from the invoice itself, less what has already been received against it, so the link and the document the client is reading can never disagree about what is owed — and an invoice the app is blocking cannot be sent for payment at all.

> **⚠ IF THE CHECK FAILS IT SAYS SO, AND IT WILL NOT CLAIM THE MONEY IS ABSENT.** A failed status check reads *"Money may already have arrived; this is not a statement that it has not."* That is deliberate. It also does not count as having checked, so the next time you open the client it tries again rather than going quiet for ten minutes over a blip.

> **Setting it up — once, and not on any device.** One Script Property on the same Apps Script everything else uses: `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`. **⚠ It must never be typed into the app's Settings** — this page is served publicly, so anything stored on a device is readable by anyone. The old *Stripe Publishable Key* and *Stripe Secret Key — via Apps Script URL* boxes are gone for that reason.
>
> **Run `testStripeAuth` in the Apps Script editor first.** It proves the key and the account *without creating a link*, and it tells you whether ACH is actually switched on for the account — a key that authenticates against an account with ACH off fails later and further away, where it reads as an app bug.
>
> **⚠ Not yet proven live (2026-09-30).** A test ACH payment is still owed, one of Anthony's own items in the workflow tracker: one link made and paid end to end proves, against the live account, that the link takes bank transfers only and that the settled payment records itself. Confirm at the same time that the account's ACH limit covers your largest deposit.

## 9. Client Dashboard

**Tab: Client Dashboard** → press a client's row, or press **+ Add New Client** at the top of the list to create one (§4). Shows full job status, the job timeline, referral source / partner, and the standing job facts. **Every action that advances the job is on the timeline** — see §9a. The heading line above it carries the three that are not steps: **✎ Edit Client**, **🔍 Walkthrough** and **📁 Drive**.

Under the timeline: **Probate Information** on any estate administered through probate (the case number, the court deadline, the property sale, the executor's authorization, the probate attorney and the executor, and at its foot the **probate package** row with **📧 Send probate package**, below), or, since 2026-10-02 (P18), **Trust Information** in its place on a trust-only matter (the trustee's attorney and the trustee, and at its foot the **trust package** row with **📧 Send trust package**, below). **Since 2026-10-03 (P19)** the Trust card carries the trust's own paper, the **Certification of Trust**, as the Probate card carries the Letters: its chip beside the heading (*Certification of Trust pending — blocker*, *received* or *not required*) and the field beside the trustee; and, under the heading, the trust itself (*Trust name*, *Trust dated*, *Trustee accepted*, each *not recorded* where blank) with the *Property sale* answer. On a pour-over will (*Both*) the Probate card shows the trust's details under its court grid. Both cards list every co-representative under *Co-Personal Representatives* or *Co-Trustees*, with the one line that says how each signs the agreement beside the representative: in DocuSign, which needs their email, or on the printed page when it is signed by hand (since 2026-10-05; until then the line said a DocuSign envelope went to the representative alone), and show the **Form 706** date where a return applies: *Form 706 due …*, or *Form 706, if a return is filed, due …* while the 706 question is unanswered, in red inside 30 days of it and once it has passed (*passed: confirm with counsel it was filed or extended*). It is a reminder: counsel or the estate's accountant files the return or its extension. The client card names the paper too (on an Estate Settlement, beside the representative) and lists the co-representatives; **Change Orders** (below); and the **Hours Log** — hours logged against the estimate plus any accepted change orders, the log itself, and **+ Log Hours Today**, which opens the Job Plan with its hours fold open.

> **⚠⚠ SEND PROBATE PACKAGE IS A REAL SEND NOW (2026-10-01; Anthony). Until this date *📧 Send Package* sent nothing** — it flipped a mark on the client and read *✓ Package Sent*, whoever pressed it and whatever had gone. The row at the foot of the Probate card says what the package carries and to whom, and one press of **📧 Send probate package** makes **one Gmail draft in your Havellin mailbox**. It goes from the Probate card, so on an estate administered through probate (matter type *Probate* or *Both*, or a Probate service whose matter is unanswered). **Since 2026-10-02 (P18; Anthony) a trust-only matter (matter type *Trust*) sends the same package, the *trust package*, from a card of its own:** *Trust Information*, the Probate card renamed, with the estate attorney's fields headed *Trustee’s Attorney*, the representative's headed *Trustee*, and **📧 Send trust package** at its foot. It shows no case number or court deadline: those are the court record, which a trust matter does not have. (Until 2026-10-03 it showed no property-sale answer or authorization either; since P19 a trust is asked the sale question, and its activation waits on the Certification of Trust, whose chip and field the card carries.) Until then a trust matter had no card and no package. A matter recorded *Neither*, an Estate Settlement whose matter is unanswered, and a living client have neither card and no package (the send refuses there too), so print their documents from the desk:
>
> | | The probate package, and the trust package |
> |---|---|
> | To | The estate attorney (Edit Client, *Estate Attorney*), copying the personal representative and `agreements@`. **Trust package:** the trustee’s attorney (the same field), copying the trustee and `agreements@`; with no attorney’s email recorded, the trustee (Edit Client, *Authorized Representative*), copying `agreements@` alone. |
> | Attached, as PDFs | Where the engagement states values, the **Court Inventory**, with the **Trust Schedule** as well on a pour-over will (matter type *Both*), and the **Estate Inventory Report**; on a *Contents list* engagement the **Contents List** in their place, and at tier *None* none of them. At every tier, the **Disposition Ledger** (since 2026-10-03, P19: where each item went and what it brought, filed under the name the close files it under, so Drive holds one; with nothing on the inventory yet it is left out, and the notice says so) and the **Appraisal Worklist**. Each is the page the desk prints, so a document with gaps goes out as it stands — DRAFT, IN PROGRESS, a total that is a floor — and never as FINAL. **Trust package:** the **Trust Schedule** in place of the Court Inventory, which it never carries. |
> | Linked in the email | Each appraisal report, offer or receipt filed on the Job Plan, named for what it is since 2026-10-06 (*Offer — Dealer bid.pdf*; one filed before then keeps *Appraisal report or offer*); the *As-Found Record* and *Estate Inventory* photograph folders, **shared with the attorney** (view only) as the send runs, by the same action as *Share w/ Counsel*, and since 2026-10-06 (P22) with the representative the package copies too, so the links do not refuse them (never `agreements@`; a copied address that could not be shared is named in the notice); every share is recorded on the client and listed on the package row and the desk (§10a); and a filed record, **Release Approvals and Chain of Custody**: who signed the written release approval for each item and on what date (since 2026-10-05, each signer with their own day), and every custody event. A folder that could not be shared is named in the email as *not yet shared with you*, and in the notice, never linked. **Trust package:** the same links, the folders shared with whoever it is addressed to and with the trustee it copies. |
> | Filed to Drive | Every attached document and the record, into *Estate Inventory*, under undated names, so the next package replaces them (§15). |
> | Recorded | Like any document send: the draft on the card (when, who, which mailbox, to whom), then **✓ I’ve sent it**, which records the send and who made it. If Gmail fails, a plain email opens instead with every document linked from Drive (it can carry no attachment), recorded as an email opened, never as sent. Both packages are one record on the client. |
> | Out of date | A draft made before the inventory changed (a line added, edited or removed, an appraisal report filed), or before the matter or the tier changed what is owed, is named on the card (a Gmail draft: *delete it, don’t send it*) and cannot be confirmed; send a fresh one. A discount never touches it: it carries no price. A trust matter re-recorded as *Both* owes the Court Inventory too, so its draft is out of date and the next package goes from the Probate card. A matter re-recorded as *Neither* takes the card away; since 2026-10-06 (P22) a draft still outstanding is named where the card was: *"An inventory package Gmail draft from Oct 3 (…) to ann@… was made before the matter changed, and this matter has no package now. Delete it in Gmail; don’t send it."* (until then nothing on screen named it). |
> | Refused | By name, with everything missing at once: the attorney’s email, the client’s Drive folder, the Apps Script URL. The card says so before anybody presses. It reads the inventory from the sheet first, and refuses rather than send an unread one as empty. **Trust package:** an email for the trustee’s attorney or for the trustee, naming where each is entered, in place of the attorney’s. |
>
> The email reads: *“Dear [attorney’s first name], Attached are Havellin’s inventory documents for the Estate of [name], [address], as they stand on [date]: [the documents]. In the estate’s Google Drive folder: [the links]. If you need anything further for the filing, reply here and we will send it.”* On a *Contents list* engagement it adds *“Valuing the property is not part of Havellin’s engagement on this estate, so the Contents List is attached in place of a valued schedule.”* At tier *None* it adds *“On this estate the inventory and the filing are your office’s; Havellin’s records of the property are below.”* **The trust package reads:** *“Dear [the trustee’s attorney’s first name, or the trustee’s when it goes to the trustee], Attached are Havellin’s inventory documents for the [name] trust administration, [address], as they stand on [date]: [the documents]. In the trust’s Google Drive folder: [the links]. If you need anything further for the administration of the trust, reply here and we will send it.”* — no case number, no *Estate of*, nothing about a court or a filing. On a *Contents list* engagement it adds *“Valuing the property is not part of Havellin’s engagement on this trust administration, so the Contents List is attached in place of a valued schedule.”* At tier *None* it adds *“On this trust administration the schedule of the trust’s property is prepared by the trustee or their counsel; Havellin’s records of the property are below.”* A job marked *Package Sent* before this date shows *Marked sent by hand before 2026-10-01*: nothing went behind that mark.

> **What was typed is shown as typed (2026-09-30).** The client card, the Probate Information card and the Hours Log show every typed field — the name, the address, the concierge, the representative, phones and emails, the notes, the case number and the attorney, an hours entry's activity — as text: a note reading *&lt;b&gt;call first&lt;/b&gt; &amp; ask for Joan* prints exactly that, and a note typed over several lines keeps its lines. Until then they went onto the page as markup, so that note printed in bold with its ampersand gone. The concierge's name, contact line and bio on the client estimate and on every invoice (typed on the Contractors tab) are text in the same way.

> **⚠ THE HEADER'S BUTTON BAR IS GONE, AND WHAT REPLACED IT IS A THREE-BUTTON BAR (2026-09-11; the third since 2026-09-19).** The dashboard used to open with a white bubble carrying seven controls — *Estimate · Submit for Approval · Client Accepted — Mark Won · Change Order · Edit Client · Drive · Activate Job*. **Five of those were already reachable further down the same screen**, which is what Anthony asked about: *"do we need the 5 boxes at the top at all? like the activate job button? estimate probably doesn't need to be there if it's down below in the timeline."* Four are rows on the timeline with their own buttons, and *Change Order* has its own **+ New** in the Change Orders card, beside the count. **⚠ And two of the copies could disagree.** The bubble offered **Activate Job** on every job; the timeline's own *Job active* row withholds that button when the activation is blocked, deliberately, because the fix is a phone call to the executor and a button that just alerts the blocker back at you is worse than none. So a contested matter with the Letters outstanding showed no button on the rail and a button at the top. What survived sits on the **Job Timeline & Payments** heading line: **✎ Edit Client** and **📁 Drive**, joined on 2026-09-19 by **🔍 Walkthrough** (§9c). **The rule is: the timeline holds every step, that bar holds only what is not one** — and a test walks the whole lifecycle and fails if anything in the bar is also offered by the rail.

> **It is deliberately NOT inside the tan *Next* card.** That card means *the one thing to do next*; hanging standing tools in it costs it exactly that meaning. It sits on the card's heading line instead, above the schedule strip and the band.

### The client list — the figures, Win / Loss, six filters and the sort

The Client Dashboard opens on the list of clients. Top to bottom: **+ Add New Client**; the four pipeline figures (*Total Jobs · Active Jobs · Pipeline Value · RE Potential*); the **Win / Loss** row directly under them (*Won · Lost · Conversion Rate · Lost Revenue*); the six filters; and the table of clients, where pressing a row opens that client.

> **⚠⚠ WIN / LOSS WAS ITS OWN TAB UNTIL 2026-09-23, AND NOTHING IT SHOWED WAS DROPPED.** The four figures moved here as a second row of tiles under Total Jobs, one tile gap below the first so the eight read as one block. **Won** and **Lost** carry a caret (▸): press either to open the list of clients behind the figure, under the row; press it again to shut it. Both can be open at once, Won above Lost.
>
> | Press | What opens |
> |---|---|
> | **Won** | Every client who said yes — client and street, service, concierge, **when and how they accepted** (the Won button's own options: email reply, phone call, text, in person), the Havellin value, and where the job is now. Newest acceptance first. *The Won list is new; the tab never had one.* |
> | **Lost** | The **loss reasons** broken out (count, share, estimated revenue lost), then the **lost prospects** themselves with reason, note and date. Newest loss first. |
>
> Every row on either list opens that client. **The lists stay open while you are in a client and come back** — open Lost, press a prospect, press **← Back to Clients**, and Lost is still open. That lasts until you reload; which list is open is not saved and does not reach the other device.

> **Closed — Deposit Retained counts as WON and is on the Won list, named as one.** The client accepted, paid something and then walked, so it produced revenue — which is why that status exists apart from Lost. The list prints its status rather than folding it in among the jobs that ran to the end. **⚠ Since 2026-09-29 it counts what it KEPT, not what it was quoted** (workflow audit M4): a job quoted $9,000 that paid a $4,500 deposit and walked adds $4,500 to the Won total, and its row reads *$4,500 · retained*. The quote never came in; the $4,500 did. **Less any refund recorded, since 2026-10-01**: an hourly walkaway that paid more than it earned gives the excess back (§9, *Closing out a job that dies*), and once that refund is recorded the row and the total count what was kept.

> **Conversion Rate reads a dash until a client has been won or lost.** It used to print **0%** over nothing decided, which with one undecided client on the books reads as *we have converted nobody*. One won and none lost is 100%; one lost and none won really is 0%, and that zero is kept.
>
> **An unread list reads dashes, never zeros, across BOTH rows.** On a device with nothing cached, until the sheet answers, every figure is an em dash and the list says *Loading clients…*; if the sheet cannot be reached it says so and tells you to check the connection and the Sync URL, then **reload the page**. "0 won, 0 lost" is a claim, and the app has not read anything yet.

**The six filters**, in this order (Anthony, 2026-09-23: *"it starts with all, and then goes active, pending approval, unassigned TC, closed and lost"*):

| Filter | Shows |
|---|---|
| All | Every client except the lost ones |
| Active | Jobs actually running |
| Pending Approval | Estimates waiting on a manager PIN — including a won client's revised price, which reads *Won · Pending Re-approval* |
| Unassigned TC | Every client not marked Lost with no concierge assigned — closed ones included |
| Closed | Finished jobs — **and Closed — Deposit Retained, which it used to leave out** (the old button tested *Closed* alone, so a retained deposit was only reachable through All) |
| Lost | The lost prospects. Every other filter leaves them out. |

**Every heading on the client table sorts.** Press a heading once for its natural direction, twice to reverse it, a third time to clear it and go back to the list's own order (newest client first). The heading shows a faint ↕ until it is the one sorting, then a bronze ↑ or ↓.

| Heading | First press |
|---|---|
| Client / Property · TC | A→Z |
| Service | In the order the service list uses — Home Prep, Home Editing, Home Transition, Home Cleanout, Estate Settlement, Probate, Contested Probate — **not** alphabetically, so each kind of job sits together and related kinds sit side by side |
| Havellin Est. · Total Est. | Largest first |
| Start Date | Soonest first |
| Status | In lifecycle order — New, Pending Approval, Approved, Won, Active, Closed, Deposit Retained, Lost |
| RE | The ★ clients first, then the unanswered, then the no's |

> **⚠ Eight filter buttons went, and every one of them is a heading now.** *New* is the top of a **Status** sort; *RE Potential* is the top of an **RE** sort; the six service buttons (*Home Editing / Transition, Home Cleanout, Estate Settlement, Probate, Contested Probate, Home Prep*) are a **Service** sort, where each one's clients sit together. Anthony: *"be able to sort by the service column, which wipes out the need for all of the big job type buttons."* A sort and a filter combine — Active sorted by Service is the running jobs grouped by type.
>
> **A blank sorts LAST whichever way the column runs** — no concierge, no price, no start date. It is a gap, not the smallest value, and reversing a sort never brings the gaps to the top. The sort lasts until you reload, like the filter.

> **Field mode leaves the Win / Loss row off.** The report was never a field tab and is desk reading; the pipeline figures, the filters and the list are all still there on the phone.

### 9a. The job timeline — where the whole job is run

Opening a client draws a **timeline** of sixteen milestones, from intake to final payment. On a desk it runs left to right in two legs, breaking at *Agreement signed*; on a phone it runs top to bottom. They are two renderings of one thing and can never disagree about state.

> **⚠ Build Estimate is reached from HERE and nowhere else, as of 2026-09-23** — the tab is gone (§1). So it has to be on the band whenever the estimate is the next thing, and that was not true of one row: the *Walkthrough* step stays lit until **noon** on the walkthrough date, and its only button used to be *Change the walkthrough date*. On the morning of a walkthrough — standing in the house — there would have been no way onto the estimate at all. A **booked** walkthrough's brown button is now **Build estimate**, because building the estimate IS the walkthrough; an unbooked one still asks for the date first and offers the estimate beside it.

**Exactly one step is ever lit** — as the current step, or as blocked. That is the promise the timeline exists to keep: open a client, and the one thing to do next is on screen. Completed steps go green; a blocked one goes red, and the band above it states both the reason and the fix, on screen rather than in a tooltip (a tooltip cannot be reached on an iPad at all). On a **closed** job a midpoint that was never paid, or never sent, wears an **amber ring**: open, not lit, and saying what settles it (below).

| Milestone | What the band offers while it is the lit step |
|---|---|
| Client intake | Recorded at intake. |
| Walkthrough | With a walkthrough date booked: **Build estimate** (the brown button) and **Change the walkthrough date** beside it. With none booked: **Set the walkthrough date**, and **Build estimate** beside it. |
| Estimate built | **Build estimate** — opens the Build Estimate screen on this job (§5). |
| Estimate approved | **Submit for approval**; once submitted, **Manager approval** (the PIN) and **Deny**. With a room in scope still unscored the step is blocked, names the rooms and offers **Open Build Estimate**. **✎ Edit estimate** sits in the tray on all four estimate steps until the signing packet goes out, and not while the estimate is with the manager (below). |
| Estimate sent to client | **✉ Send estimate** → **✓ I've sent it**. Plus View / Print / the Drive copy, and **Offer discount** once the estimate is approved, until the signing packet goes out. Opens again, reading **✉ Send revised estimate**, when the approved price rises above the one that went (below). |
| Client accepted | **✓ Client accepted — mark won**, with **Mark lost** beside it. After a raise it reads **✓ Client accepted the revised price** (below). |
| Signing packet sent | **✉ Send for signature — DocuSign**, with **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand** beside it until the packet has gone (§8a). DocuSign records the send itself, so there is no *I've sent it*; the PDF route makes a Gmail draft and asks for **✓ I've sent it** like the other documents. **🤝 Handed over in person** (since 2026-10-01) is beside both, for a printed packet given to the client: it asks, records the send, and the step reads *Handed over in person* once done (§8). |
| Agreement signed | While a DocuSign envelope is out the row reads *DocuSign is watching for it* and the button is **↻ Check DocuSign now**; the signature records itself (§8a). On the PDF route, or once the packet was handed over in person, **✓ Record the signed agreement** (§8). |
| Deposit invoice sent | **✉ Send deposit invoice**, **🏦 ACH payment link** (§8b). |
| Deposit received | **✓ Record payment**, with **🏦 ACH payment link** beside it until the deposit is in — after a part cheque the link asks only for the balance (§8b). **Blocked, in red, when a void has taken the deposit below its target** (since 2026-10-01), even on an active job: the band reads *Deposit voided: record the replacement payment*, with the payment, the day, the reason and the fix printed under it, and the same **✓ Record payment**. The job stays active and nothing rewinds (§8, *The payments list*). |
| Job active | **▶ Activate job**. Blocks on the Letters on any estate administered through probate — the matter type decides, not the service (§4) — and, since 2026-10-03 (P19), on the successor trustee's Certification of Trust on a trust-only matter; the band names the paper and whom to chase. Once it goes through you land on **that client's Job Plan** (2026-09-23), and pressing it before the target start asks first (§9a-i). From here on, **■ Close job** sits beside every lit step as an outline button (2026-09-29, below). **Once done, the step shows the day the job was activated and the name that activation recorded** (2026-09-30; until then it was the one done step with no date on it): the job's assigned concierge for an activation from 2026-09-30, and the manager who approved the price for an earlier one or on a job with nobody assigned. |
| Midpoint invoice sent | The same send pair — the filled button from the plan's **halfway day**, once half the work is done, or once **every room is locked**, whichever comes first. Before then the band says *Do the work* and the send is an outline button reading *due around &lt;date&gt;* (below). **On Home Prep this row is *Second invoice sent***, and its payment row *Second payment*: that engagement has no project midpoint, and its estimate bills the second 25% once the vendor schedule is booked. |
| Midpoint payment | **✓ Record payment** at that stage. An unpaid midpoint on a closed job stays **open**, and **✓ Record midpoint payment** stays in the strip (below). |
| Work complete | **■ Close job** — stamps the handover date. The filled button here; an outline beside every lit step from *Job active* on. A job that was closed and re-opened reads *Re-opened — the earlier close was undone* here until it is closed again. |
| Final invoice sent | The same send pair, with **🔑 Manager approval** beside it. The PIN is needed only when the final is outside ±15% (§12) — pressed inside, it says so, and pressed on an hourly final with no hours logged it says what blocks it (since 2026-09-30, P16) — and until it is given the final's View, Print and Send refuse with the reason. It is the filled button **the moment the job is closed**, whatever the midpoint rows say, with **↺ Re-open job** beside it as an outline until the final goes out (below). **On an hourly job with no hours logged the final has no View or Print** (2026-09-29) — it cannot be priced, and both used to be offered and then refuse on the press; once the job is closed the row says *No hours are logged yet — log them on the Job Plan and the final can be priced*. **Crew hours logged with no concierge hours are flagged on the row** from the close until the final goes out (since 2026-09-30, P16): *“60.0 crew hours logged with no concierge hours. The concierge is on site for every crew hour, so this final is likely under-billing $12,000 of concierge time. Check the log before sending.”* A flag, never a refusal (§12). |
| Final payment | **✓ Record payment** at that stage. |

> **The action button is drawn in the pinned band and nowhere else.** The lit row and the band are the same step, so drawing it twice would put one control on screen twice and make you check which is real. The rows themselves are a status read. Anything else the job allows out of sequence gathers into one strip at the foot: **✎ Edit estimate** and **Offer discount** until the signing packet goes out, **✓ Record midpoint payment** on a closed job, and the links to every document not in the band. (Edit Client is on the heading line and Mark lost beside *Client accepted*; neither is ever in the strip.)
>
> **⚠ The strip holds the other documents, not the one in play (changed 2026-09-13).** The document for the stage you are on is in the band at the top, in the tray (§9a-ii). The strip carries every other document that is ready: those from earlier stages — it is the only on-screen route back to them — and those ahead of the job that can already be read: the signing packet, to read only, once the estimate is approved, and the deposit, midpoint and final invoices from the client's yes (the final once it can be priced). Nothing is offered in both places — the strip is built from what the band already took. On a job whose estimate is still a draft it is now legitimately **empty**, because every link it used to carry was either moved into the tray or refused by the readiness gate.
>
> **On a finished job the tray keeps the last document sent — the final invoice.** The band reads **COMPLETE** over *Last sent — Invoice — Final* with *View final invoice*, *Print final invoice* and *Filed final invoice*, and the strip holds the four documents before it. Decided with Anthony on 2026-09-23, who first asked for the final to be added to the strip and then, told that meant taking it out of the band: *"the big buttons are always the most current stage."* So the final is in the big buttons, once, and not in the strip.

> **The timeline is driven by the five things that actually carry a job's state, not by the status label.** The status word on a client's row and the header chip is one field that estimate events write, and until 2026-09-29 they overwrote it whatever the job had reached — re-approving a won client's estimate knocked it back to *Approved*. Since then an estimate event never moves an active or closed job, and a won job stays won through a re-price (§9a-ii), but the rule stands: each milestone reads whichever of the five owns it, so the timeline stays right whatever the status word says. **Trust the timeline over the status chip.**

> **The red ⚠ chips beside the status are the activation gates** — *Agreement not signed*, *Deposit not received*, *Executor auth pending* — read off the rule **Activate Job** enforces, and shown until the job is active; each chip's fix is printed under the chips, one line each (since 2026-10-06, P22; until then it was only the chip's tooltip, which an iPad cannot reach), and the lit timeline row prints its own. **Since 2026-09-30 (P16) *Agreement not signed* says what is actually missing**: the estimate or the client's yes first, in the words the packet's own gate uses; then *“Send the signing packet from the timeline, then record the signature once the client signs.”*; then *“The signing packet is out: record the signature on the timeline once the client has signed.”* — or, with a DocuSign envelope out, that DocuSign is watching for the client's signature and records it when the envelope completes. It said *“Approve the agreement, send the signing packet, then record the signature”* on every job, though nobody has approved an agreement by hand since 2026-09-10: the first print or send does it (§8).

> **A step can be done out of order and the timeline copes.** Some steps can be recorded out of order — a deposit paid through the ACH link before its invoice was confirmed as sent, or a job carried over from the retired tabs with no estimate-sent date. Later steps stay green and the light lands on the *earliest* gap rather than treating everything after it as unfinished. (This note used to name *Estimate sent to client* as a step somebody could skip; from the dashboard it cannot be — *Client accepted* is not offered until it is done.)

> **⚠⚠ A higher price asks the client again (2026-09-29).** Anthony, deciding it: if a won client's estimate is edited up and re-approved, the acceptance is asked again. The app records two figures — the price on the estimate when you press *I've sent it*, and the price the client accepted when you press *Client accepted* — and when a manager approves the estimate at a price **above** either of them:
>
> - **Above the price sent:** *Estimate sent to client* opens again and the band reads **Send revised estimate**, both before the client has said yes and after. Without that, the acceptance recorded next would be at a figure they never saw.
> - **Above the price accepted:** *Client accepted* opens again and reads **Client accepted the revised price**, with **Mark lost** beside it — a client can say no to a higher price. The pop-up names the figure they accepted and the one they are being asked about, and **Record Acceptance** keeps the earlier yes beside the new one.
> - **The job stays Won.** Staffing, the Job Plan and Win / Loss are untouched; the status reads **Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance** (§9). The signing packet, the deposit invoice and the Stripe link wait for the new yes; the packet may be read on screen, marked PREVIEW, and not printed or sent.
> - **The manager is told where the PIN was typed**, and the concierge's *Estimate Approved* email carries the same sentence.
> - **A discount never reopens anything** — it only lowers what the client agreed to — and **nothing reopens once the signing packet has gone out**: the client is reading and signing the current price.
> - **A client who was won, marked lost and came back** is asked a first yes, not a re-acceptance: the old yes was withdrawn when they walked away.
>
> **⚠ A job recorded before 2026-09-29 carries neither figure and is asked nothing** — there is no honest number to compare. An estimate *sent* before then and *accepted* after it reopens the acceptance but not the send; the manager's notice and the concierge's email both say to send the revised estimate first, so do that before asking.

> **⚠ *Edit estimate* waits for the manager (2026-09-29).** While an estimate is out for approval it is locked on Build Estimate, and the timeline used to offer *Edit estimate* anyway, landing you on a screen reading *Out for Manager Approval*. Anthony: *"hide until PIN."* It is not offered on any row until the manager approves or denies, and pressing it by any other route is refused with the same reason. The manager's PIN is the next move on the timeline.

> **A dead job collapses to one row.** *Lost* and *Closed — Deposit Retained* are terminal, so there is no next action and nothing is lit — a button there would invite a click the app refuses. On an hourly *Closed — Deposit Retained* job the card carries the **Walkaway settlement** under the row, with **Record refund** while a refund is due (since 2026-10-01; *Closing out a job that dies*, below).

> **⚠⚠ Close job is offered from the day the job is active, and the final invoice does not wait for the midpoint (2026-09-29).** The rows run *Midpoint invoice sent → Midpoint payment → Work complete → Final invoice*, and *Close job* used to live on *Work complete* alone. So a finished job whose midpoint invoice had gone out unpaid read *Collect the midpoint payment* with **no way to close it and no way to send the final**: the only way out was to record money that had not arrived. The client list's Status button, which looked like a second route, had never rendered at all (§9). Decided with Anthony off the 2026-09-28 workflow audit:
>
> - **■ Close job is an outline button beside the lit step on every step from *Job active* on.** On *Work complete* it is the filled button, as before, and it still refuses while any vendor used is unrated (§11).
> - **With no midpoint payment recorded it asks first**: *Close this job now?*, saying that the final invoice bills everything not yet paid, that today is recorded as the handover date, and that **Re-open** can undo the close until the final invoice goes out. Cancel changes nothing. With a midpoint payment on file it asks nothing, as before — except, since 2026-10-03 (P19), on an estate whose Disposition Ledger has no signed copy on file: the same one question names it (§10a, *Close-out records*), and OK still closes. Closing also files the ledger to Drive.
> - **The moment the job is closed, the final invoice is the band's filled button**, whatever the midpoint rows say. That is safe because the final reconciles against the payments actually received (§12): an unpaid midpoint's share is simply still owed, and the final bills it.
> - **The midpoint rows stay open until they are settled**, with an amber ring rather than a light. The payment row reads *Unpaid — the final invoice carries it* and **✓ Record midpoint payment** stays in the strip, so a midpoint cheque that arrives later is recorded normally and the row goes green. Once the final is paid it reads *Paid with the final invoice*, because its share was inside that balance.
