Home Prep for Sale has no rooms and no camera: its plan is the vendor and budget view (§6c), and its Job Admin & Inv tab carries no inventory (§10a).

> **Corrected 2026-08-03 — this said *Active*, and the app used to enforce it.** Photo capture now opens as soon as the job is **Won**, matching the gate the Job Plan screen itself enforces. It mattered: a job sits at *Won* for the entire stretch between the client accepting and the deposit being recorded, the Job Plan renders its room cards — camera buttons included — that whole time, and every shot taken in that window was **silently discarded**. No upload, no record, no message: the camera opened, the photo was taken, and the app did nothing. Day-one walkthrough photos evaporated. **If either gate ever moves, move both.**

> **Three refusals that were silent now speak.** A job that isn't Won, a job that can't be resolved, and a failed photo whose image data is no longer held all now say so. A camera button that does nothing is indistinguishable from one that worked, which is why these were worth more than a log line.

> **⚠⚠ A JOB PLAN STAGE THAT SHUT ITSELF WITHIN SECONDS (fixed 2026-09-11).** Reported from a job: *"when i expand a job plan stage, it automatically shuts on me within seconds. i can't do anything."* Two faults, either of which alone made the tab unusable. **The tab was re-entering itself.** After rendering, it pulls anything another device photographed and redraws if that changed something — but "changed" was true the moment the sheet held *any* photo for the job, so on a job with photos on file it re-rendered forever, one network round trip per cycle. It was harmless until there were photographs in the sheet to come back. Measured on the real page: **21 re-renders and 20 round trips before, one of each after.** **And any redraw shut every open phase**, because the accordion lived only in the markup — and the Job Plan legitimately redraws for several reasons (a photo landing from the other device, the estimate store arriving, a directory going stale). Which folds you have open is remembered for the session, so those redraws are invisible. Since 2026-09-20 only the two tools at the top fold (*Vendors & partners*, *Hours & daily close*); the stages of the job are always open (§11).

> **Nor across a reload (since 2026-10-06, P22).** The watchdog below lives in the page, so a shot still uploading when the page was closed, reloaded or died came back reading *Uploading…* for good, and a detail shot stuck that way had no tile at all. On the next load a shot still uploading after 90 seconds that the page is not sending reads as *⚠ not saved*, with its tile (a detail shot under *Detail shots not saved*) and its **Retry**; the image is held on the device while it uploads, so Retry can resend it.

> **⚠ A photo can no longer be stuck on *Uploading…* (2026-09-11).** A browser request has no time limit, so a Drive upload that never came back left the room card reading *Uploading…* for the rest of the job — no count, no error, no Retry, nothing to press. There is a **90-second watchdog** on it now: a request that has not answered by then is recorded as failed, says so, and points at Retry — the image is still held on the device, so it goes up as soon as the cause is fixed.

> **⚠ Any shot but an as-found one can be deleted, from the job and from Drive (2026-09-20).** Anthony, in a room: *"i took a crummy picture and i want to be able to delete it."* Until this date the only photograph you could remove was one that had failed to reach Drive, with a **✕** beside Retry. Now every thumbnail in the room workspace carries a **🗑** in its corner, and so does the last shot in the camera. It asks first, and says what goes with the shot: its detail shots, and, by name, any line split out of the photograph. The shot comes off the job straight away. If it reached Drive, the file moves to Google Drive's bin, which keeps it for 30 days; if Drive refuses, a red message says the file is off the job but still in the client's folder. *Removed items* on the Job Admin & Inv tab gives the lines back, but not the image (§10a). **As-found shots cannot be deleted** from either bin: they are the record of the room before anything was touched, and a poor frame does no harm there, so the bin refuses and says to shoot another.

> **A failed photo now survives the session.** Its **Retry** used to do nothing at all once the tab had been closed — the image was held in memory only, so a shot that failed on Tuesday was gone by Wednesday with a live-looking Retry button sitting beside it. Failed shots now keep their image data on the device (under their own storage key, so image data can never crowd out the inventory record) until they upload. If the device has no room to hold one, you are told to retry it *now*, before leaving the job.

> **Photo counts are correct on the first open of a job.** The room cards used to be drawn before the photo records were loaded, so the first time you opened any job in a session every count read zero and every failed-upload flag was hidden — photos correctly filed in Drive read on screen as photos that never happened. Related: uploads now resolve the destination through the shared folder resolver, so a job folder created before the 2026-07-15 *Photos* + *Asset Documentation* merge files correctly instead of failing every photo (§15).

### The room list and the room workspace (rebuilt 2026-09-19)

The rooms are a block of **cards, three across on a desk** (two on the iPad, one on a phone), the *In the house* card on the plan — second on the thread, after *Before Day 1*, open at all times — one card per in-scope room in walkthrough order, carrying the name and the status pill on the top line, the shot counts (*2 found · 5 items · 1 after*) under it, and the walkthrough note from the estimate on one truncated line. Rooms marked out of scope on the estimate are named in a single *Not in scope — homeowner is handling* line and are not rows. Tapping a card opens the **room workspace** as a **pop-up over the plan** — a dialog no wider than 760px on a desk, dimmed plan behind it, closed by the *← Rooms* button, a tap outside it or Esc; on a phone it is the whole screen — with three camera buttons (**As found · Items · After**), the **As-found pass complete** tick under them, the **Lock** and **Cleared** taps, and a strip of the room's shots grouped by pass, each with a 🗑 and, on a failed one, **Retry**. A detail shot is counted on its item's tile (*+1 detail*) and has a tile of its own only if it failed to reach Drive, under *Detail shots not saved*, naming the item it is a close-up of — since 2026-09-30; until then a failed detail shot was drawn nowhere, so its Retry and 🗑 were out of reach once the camera moved on, while the room card read *⚠ not saved* for good. The old per-room cards — two grids, five statuses, a typed name and category before every shot — are gone.

> **Why the rooms are never inside a fold — and no longer at the top either.** Every stage started closed, and the first cut put the room list inside Phase 1 — so the whole field surface sat behind a tap on a bar reading *Phase 1*, which the browser check found when it could not click a room at all. That morning's fix put the rooms above every fold, and Anthony read it, correctly, as out of order: the vendor calls and the pre-job call come before the rooms in a real job. The rooms now sit in their place in the order (§11), open at all times — and since 2026-09-20 so does every other stage of the job: only the two tools above it, *Vendors & partners* and *Hours & daily close*, fold. The rooms are the work; the stages are the checklist, and neither is behind a bar.

> **The intake brief is in the room now (new 2026-09-19).** The *Standing job flags* brief — every must-find with its **Found it** tick, the safety answer, cash, safes, documents, the lot — is the first thing in the workspace's scrolling body, above the pass instructions and the three cameras, so whoever is shooting the study reads *"the coin collection is in the study safe"* standing in the study rather than two taps back on the plan header. The red firearms line stays pinned above the body, where a scroll cannot hide it, so the brief in the room carries no second copy of it; a job whose only flag is firearms shows the pinned line and nothing else.

### Three passes per room, and nothing typed in the field

| Pass | What is shot | What it becomes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **As found** | Wide shots from the corners, then every drawer, cabinet, closet and box opened and photographed where it sits, undisturbed. | Evidence of what the house held on Day 1. Filed as `before` in the client's **As-Found Record** folder; never an inventory line, and never deleted. **Mandatory on estate and probate jobs**: *Lock* is refused until at least one exists **and** the *As-found pass complete* box under the cameras is ticked (the tick since 2026-09-20; `lockRefusal`, keyed on `isDecedentJob`). The app cannot count drawers, so a person attests the pass: the tick records the date and the job's concierge, and can be unticked. On a living-client job either gap is an amber flag (`lockFlag`). |
| **Items** | After sorting: one shot per object, shelf or obvious lot, with one chip lit — **Keep · New home · To a person · Donate · Sell · Remove · Undecided** — and, per shot, **⚑ Appraise**, **Detail of last** and a spoken or typed note. *New home* is offered only where the job has one to go to (a Home Transition, or any job with a new address recorded); *To a person* is offered on every job, and on an estate it is the bequest release. (Five chips until 2026-09-21.) | One inventory line per shot to start with (at the desk one photograph can carry several lines, §10a), with **no object name and the default category**. The chip maps onto the desk's nine dispositions (New home → Move, To a person → Distribute, Remove → Junk, Undecided → blank). A *detail* shot files under the previous item (`groupId`) and is not a line. The note lands as `fieldNote`. |
| **After** | The empty room. | Filed as `after`. *Cleared* flags without one. |

- **The chip latches; Appraise and Detail reset.** A disposition is a standing answer about the shelf being worked; *send this one to a specialist* and *this is a close-up of the last one* are judgements about a single object, so both clear after every shot rather than latching on and quietly flagging the rest of the room. **Undecided is the default**, so an item nobody decided in the house lands in *Not yet decided* at the top of the Job Admin & Inv tab, which is the honest place for it.
- **The camera stays open until Done.** It is the app's own viewfinder (`getUserMedia`), not the phone's camera app — the native picker closes after every shot and cannot be kept open, which is the whole reason the in-app camera exists. Where camera access is refused the same overlay degrades to the native picker one shot at a time and says so; every shot still files.
- **Every shot uploads as it is taken.** As-found shots file to the client's **As-Found Record** folder (since 2026-09-20; a job folder made before then has none, so they go to **Estate Inventory** until somebody creates *As-Found Record* in it by hand in Drive, which the app then picks up). Item, detail and after shots file to **Estate Inventory**. The 90-second watchdog and Retry work as before, and any shot but an as-found one can be deleted (above). The manifest sync is scheduled for items and details.
- **Filename:** `HVL-2609-K7QX_Kitchen_INV_3_2026-09-19_184821.jpg` — job, room, pass (`before` / `INV` / `DETAIL` / `after`), sequence within the room and pass, timestamp. It used to carry the typed object name and category; it carries neither now, because neither exists in the field.
- **The note on the last shot saves itself; there is no Add button (2026-09-20).** A typed box is always there (*Note on the last shot — saved automatically*), with **🎤 Hold to talk** beside it where the browser offers speech recognition. A spoken note is saved the moment you let go. A typed note is saved by Enter, by the next shutter or by **Done**, and it goes on the shot that was last when you started typing, even if you shoot the next one before you finish. Either way the words are added to that shot's note, never written over it. Until this date the app's own **Done** threw a typed note away (the phone keyboard's *done* key saved it), and the Add button was one more step.

### Room status: three states, two taps

A room is **pending**, **locked** (sorted and shot — the decisions are made) or **cleared** (the specialists have finished, it is empty and the after shot is taken). *Cleared* is offered only on a locked room. Both taps live in the room workspace beside the camera. The five-state ladder (*pending / sorting / locked / packed / complete*) and the two grids that enforced it are gone: a plan written on the old build reads *sorting* as pending and *packed* or *complete* as cleared, on read, without rewriting the stored record (`roomStatusNormalize`). **Work done** on the Client Dashboard earns a room's concierge hours at *locked* and its specialist hours at *cleared*, exactly as it used to at *packed*.

### Notable Collections — Asset Documentation

Each collection flagged on the estimate has a card under **Collection Partners** in the Job Plan's *Vendors & partners* fold, with two buttons:

- **📷 Capture Asset Photo** — condition and identification photography, filed to **Estate Inventory** as `HVL-YYMM-XXXX_ASSET_<Collection>_<n>_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<HHMMSS>.jpg`.
- **📄 Upload Appraisal**, **📄 Upload Offer**, **📄 Upload Receipt** — an appraisal, a dealer's or buyer's offer, or a receipt, PDF or image, filed to **Estate Inventory** as `HVL-YYMM-XXXX_APPRSL_<Collection>_<n>_<YYYY-MM-DD>_<HHMMSS>.<ext>` (an offer `_OFFER_`, a receipt `_RCPT_`). The collection lists each as *Appraisal report*, *Offer* or *Receipt*, and the estate package links it under that name. Until 2026-10-06 (P22) one button, *Upload Appraisal / Offer*, filed all three alike, and the package linked each as *Appraisal report or offer* (one filed then still reads so).

### ⚑ Appraise — the second avenue

The toggle beside the chips is not another chip, and that is the whole design: an appraisal is not a destination, it is the step *before* one. Press it and the item goes to the Appraisal Worklist **while keeping whatever disposition you chipped** — first appraise, then auction or consign or sell. In the field the app knows nothing about the object (no name, no category), so the automatic category rule that routes art, jewelry, silver, antiques, rugs, collectibles, firearms, wine and instruments to the worklist fires **at the desk**, once the line has been named and categorised on the Job Admin & Inv tab, and only on an estate (§10a). The toggle is for what no category rule will ever see — the period side table, the unmarked bronze — and it is the only way to record that judgement while standing in front of the object. Missed in the house, *Needs Appraisal* in the item panel's Valuation section does the same thing that evening. It resets after every shot and the chip deliberately does not: a disposition is a standing answer about the shelf, this is a judgement about one object.

## 10a. Job Admin & Inventory (Tab)

**Tab: Job Admin & Inv** → select a client. A per-client tangible-personal-property manifest, and — on a decedent job — the estate documentation workspace. The manifest itself runs on **every** labour service; only the fiduciary half is estate-only (see below). It assembles from three sources: the shots taken on the Items pass in the room workspace (they arrive unnamed — see below), **+ Add line item** (an asset with no photo — cash, account, a vehicle), and **import from the estimate** (below).

> **The two intake questions sit above Job Admin (new 2026-09-19).** The desk names that evening's shots against the must-find list, so the list is there: *From intake — must find · safety*, each must-find with its **Found it** tick, then the safety answer. Only those two; cash, safes and the other ticked rows stay on the Job Plan. It renders nothing on a job with neither answer.

> **Job Admin — folded at the top of the tab (new 2026-09-19).** The desk paperwork that used to be checkboxes on the Job Plan: *Financial close* (vendor invoices filed; on disposal jobs settlement timelines confirmed; and, until 2026-10-03, proceeds reconciled and donation receipts, which are lines the app reads since P19), the *Florida court & legal compliance* list (six items, from the §733.604 inventory verified to the final accounting — seven until 2026-10-03, when the PR's sign-off box went, below — which of them render is decided by the matter type and the engagement tier, not by the service; see below), on a trust matter the trustee's own *Trust administration* list (new 2026-10-03, below) and *Records* (the seven-year archive). Above the boxes are the lines the app reads for itself — first, since 2026-10-03, any original will or cash recorded on the Job Plan's *Found on site* card (§11), in red while Havellin holds it; then rooms cleared, on an estate with property released to a person *Signed receipt for every item released to a person* (new 2026-10-03), hours logged, change orders accepted, final invoice sent, and since 2026-10-03 the Disposition Ledger signed on an estate, donation receipts line by line and sale proceeds reconciled (*Close-out records*, below) — and the fold carries the count (*0 of 3 ticked · 3 derived items still open* on a Home Cleanout with nothing done yet; it read *0 of 5* before two of its boxes became lines). It renders only on a won job and, deliberately, only here: it is inventory paperwork, and the Job Admin & Inv tab is where the concierge already is in the middle of a job. Opening it never creates a plan record.

> **Unnamed shots is the first worklist count (new 2026-09-19).** Every Items-pass shot arrives with no object name, so its row opens with an empty name box outlined in bronze (*What is it? the agent proposes this — correct it here*), and under it the category and room, a count of its detail shots (*+1 detail shot*) and the spoken field note (🎤); the item panel carries the editable note and the detail thumbnails. Until 2026-09-20 the row read *Unnamed — shot 3, name it below*, with nowhere below to name it; every row now has the box, named or not, so a name is typed or corrected in the list itself. Name it — by hand, or with **Name N shots** (below) — and it drops off the count. A detail shot is never a manifest line and is never numbered: two shots of one object are one line.

> **⚠⚠ The court list follows the CONTRACT now, not the service type (new 2026-09-21).** Until this date the whole seven-item compliance list keyed on the service being Probate or Contested Probate, and that was wrong in both directions at once. **An Estate Settlement administering a probate estate got no compliance list at all**, whatever it had been contracted for — five desk boxes and nothing else. And **a probate estate contracted at *None***, where the agreement says counsel does the whole inventory, **was still told to verify date-of-death FMV on every line and attach the appraisals** — two instructions nobody on the job could follow. Measured before it was changed: tier and matter type changed nothing across all twenty combinations on every one of the seven services. What decides each box now:

| Box | Renders when |
| --- | --- |
| §733.604 inventory verified — date-of-death FMV on every line | the matter is **Probate** or **Both** *and* the tier is **Inventory with values** or **Inventory + appraisals** — we state the values |
| All professional appraisals attached | the same matter test, *and* the appraisals are Havellin's to arrange: the tier is **Inventory + appraisals**, or (since 2026-09-30) the approved estimate lists an appraiser — **or the job actually has an appraiser on its roster**, whatever the tier |
| Non-probate assets excluded | the same matter test, *and* the tier produces an inventory at all (anything but **None**) |
| Served · Filed within 60 days · Final accounting | the matter test alone — the court's procedure happens whoever built the schedule |

> **The PR's sign-off box is gone (2026-10-03, P19).** *PR sign-off obtained on the final disposition summary* was a tick that said a signature existed without the paper. The representative signs off on the Disposition Ledger, and its signed copy filed to Drive is the record; the trustee's list (below) has no sign-off box either. A tick made under the old box stays on the record and is no longer drawn.

> **The same one-word fix reached the Job Plan:** *Personal Representative authority confirmed* under Before Day 1 now follows the matter type too, so an Estate Settlement on a probate matter is finally asked it and a probate job recorded as a trust administration is not. Since 2026-10-03 a trust administration is asked its own twin instead, under a *Trust gate* heading: *Certification of Trust (§736.1017) read — any limits on the trustee's powers noted*. A pour-over (*Both*) is asked both.

> **An unanswered matter type is not a trust matter, and a probate job created before the field existed keeps its checklist.** A matter recorded on the job decides. An *unanswered* one on a Probate or Contested Probate service still means a court case was open at intake — that is what the service name records — so the list every existing probate job has today survives rather than disappearing. Nothing in a service name says trust, so there is no honest fallback in that direction. **One box does change on a legacy job: *All professional appraisals attached* is withheld**, because a job carrying only the old `docScope` reads as *Inventory with values*, which does not make appraisals ours — and it comes straight back the moment an appraiser is on the roster, or (since 2026-09-30) on the approved estimate. Measured: a legacy probate job renders **11** boxes where it rendered 12 — **10** since 2026-10-03, when the PR's sign-off box went (above).

> **⚠ A trust matter gets no court list — since 2026-10-03 it gets the trustee's own — and the card says which.** Once an empty court section can mean two different things — *this estate has no probate in it* or *nobody answered the question* — the desk needs to be told which. Two lines on the Job Admin card, the last of the derived lines above the boxes, do it: **Matter type recorded** (green, naming the answer; open, reading *not recorded — the Florida court list and the trustee's list are withheld until it is. Edit Client*, which named the court list alone until 2026-10-03) and **Contracted to produce** (green, naming the tier; open, reading *not recorded — Edit Client, before the agreement goes out*). Both are **derived and never ticked**: they are recorded at intake and corrected on Edit Client, so a box here would be a second store of one fact. They render on this card and **not** on the Job Plan's Close-out stage, whose reader is nowhere near the list they describe.
>
> **The trustee's own checklist is built (new 2026-10-03, P19).** Until then this note said it was not; the **Trust Schedule** it checks was built on 2026-09-21 (*The trustee's schedule is built*, below). Anthony: *"we need full functionality even if its not a court doc."* On a matter recorded *Trust* or *Both* the card carries a *Trust administration* section, a heading with no court in it, keyed like the court list on the matter and the tier and never on the service:

| Box | Renders when |
| --- | --- |
| Trust Schedule verified — date-of-death FMV on every line | the matter is **Trust** or **Both** *and* the tier states values (**Inventory with values** or **Inventory + appraisals**) |
| All professional appraisals attached to the Trust Schedule | the same matter test, off the probate track (on **Both** the court list asks it, so it is asked once), *and* the court list's own test: the appraisals are Havellin's to arrange, or an appraiser is on the job's roster |
| Property the trust does not hold excluded (probate property, joint with survivorship, POD/TOD, beneficiary-designated) | the same matter test, *and* the tier produces an inventory at all (anything but **None**) |
| Trust Schedule delivered to the successor trustee or their counsel · Disposition records delivered for the trustee's accounting (§736.08135): sale proceeds, donations, disposal costs | the matter test alone — they happen whoever built the schedule |

> There is **no trustee sign-off box**, as there is no longer a PR's: the signed Disposition Ledger is the record. Measured on a Probate job recorded as a trust: **7** boxes at *None*, 8 at *Contents list*, 9 at *Inventory with values* and 10 at *Inventory + appraisals*, the financial close and the archive among them (it was 5 at every tier). A matter recorded *Neither* still gets neither list.

> **Renamed *Job Admin & Inv*, and the client follows you here from the Job Plan (new 2026-09-20).** The tab read *Inventory* while Job Admin had been folded at the top of it since 2026-09-19, so the name described half of what was on it. Anthony: *"job plan and job admin kind of signal what they're used for."* The nav is abbreviated to fit; the page still says Job Admin & Inventory in full. **The job picker is now shared with the Job Plan:** pick a client on either tab and the other opens on that client, so going back and forth mid-job no longer means selecting twice. It is remembered for the session only — nothing is written to the device or the sheet, and a reload clears it. A job this device no longer has is refused rather than assigned, so the picker is never left empty. **Opening a client on the Client Dashboard records it too (2026-09-23)**, so tapping Job Plan next opens that client rather than *Select a job*, and the Job Plan redraws every time you open it.

> **The top of the tab: who, where, and what to do next (new 2026-09-22).** Anthony: *"add client info like on job plan … to job admin tab. also, below that … the brown band with actions and the deposit & start timeline below."* Under the client picker the tab now opens with the **client line** (client, service, property, target start, the Drive folder, and on a labour job the schedule strip), then **the brown Next band** — the same band as the Client Dashboard, with the same buttons — then the **Deposit & Start** half of the timeline. It is the dashboard's own renderer, not a copy, so the two cannot disagree about what is next. It is there so the **midpoint invoice** is reminded where the day ends, not only on a tab nobody opens mid-job. Only the second leg of the timeline is drawn: the first leg (intake to signature) is over by the time the job is won. A button pressed here acts on **this** tab's client, even if a Client Dashboard was left open on another one, and its message lands on this tab.

> **⚠ A Home Prep job has no inventory on this tab (new 2026-09-22).** Anthony, off a prep job: *"everything below job admin – desk paperwork is immaterial to a home prep job."* It offered a Contents Record, a CSV, an Approval Request, *+ Add line item*, an empty manifest and the appraiser roster, on a house whose contents are not ours to record. On a prep job the tab is now the client line, the band, the intake's safety answer where one was recorded, **Job Admin — open by default** (it is the tab's main business there) and the **Close-out** card, and nothing else. The desk card also stopped asking a prep job questions that cannot apply: no *All rooms cleared — 0 of 0* (there are no rooms), no *Hours logged* on a fee-only engagement (it logs none), and no donation receipts. Every other service keeps its inventory exactly as before, with the Close-out card beside Job Admin.

### Agent One — naming the shots (new 2026-09-22)

Anthony, on what the field camera is for: *"the whole point is that we don't take the time to name objects in the field. we just capture per room and the agent names."* Every Items-pass shot has landed with **no object name** since the camera was rebuilt on 2026-09-19 — that was always the design, and naming them was the desk's job. This is what does the desk's half of it. **Nothing about the field changes**: the crew still types nothing.

A **Name N shots** button sits first on the Inventory Review bar, because on a freshly shot job every other button there reads off names. It reads the photographs and fills in two things — the object name and the category — plus a quantity when something is recorded as a lot. It withdraws itself once nothing is left unnamed; a frame it found empty stays unnamed and is offered again on the next run.

| It writes | It never writes |
|---|---|
| Object Name<br>Category<br>Qty (on a lot) | **Any value** — that is the valuing pass, and at the *Contents list* and *None* tiers the agreement says outright that Havellin states no opinion of value<br>**Disposition** — *Not yet decided* is the deliberate default and silence must never read as Keep<br>**NFA Item** — that flag blocks transport and has no override, so a person sets it<br>**Needs Appraisal** — already derived from the category<br>Bequest, dispute, exempt, asset track, authority, receipts — facts about the will and the matter, not about a photograph |

> **Every row comes back marked unchecked, and that is the whole gate.** A named row reads *agent* in bronze on the row, or *agent · unsure* when it was a guess, with its reasoning in the tooltip. The moment you edit the name or the category the badge clears and the row is yours. Nothing is marked reviewed by the agent, so every client document still carries the **IN PROGRESS — N of M items reviewed** stamp until a person has been through them. Review still gates nothing and withholds nothing — that rule is unchanged, so a client can still be shown honest work in progress.

> **Lot by default, itemised where it earns it.** A shelf of books, a drawer of flatware, a box of glassware comes back as *one* line with a quantity, not thirty. What gets a line of its own: the nine intrinsic categories (Art & Décor, Antiques, Jewelry & Watches, Silver & Precious Metal, Rugs & Carpets, Collectibles, Firearms, Wine & Spirits, Musical Instruments), anything with a readable maker's mark or signature, and anything visibly individual. A large estate should come back in the **hundreds** of lines rather than the thousands — which is what keeps the tab quick and the desk review a job somebody can actually finish. The §20.2031-6(a) grouping cap is untouched by all of this: the app still tests it itself once a value exists (§10a, the grouping cap).

> **⚠ It names things as fully as the photograph allows, and hedges when it is guessing.** Anthony, overruling a more cautious first draft: *"i liked that the first run you were able to guess that my speakers were B&O, because they are. and you flagged the banksy as likely a reproduction, which it is … we can always edit at desk, but more detail is helpful and we will confirm, which is our job."* So it names the maker, the model, the period and the pattern whenever it can see them. It states a thing flatly only when it is *readable* in the frame; when it is reading the shape rather than a mark it says so in the words — *"appears to be Bang & Olufsen"*, *"Banksy print, likely a reproduction"*. Both of those are useful; only one of them is a claim. **Confirming is the concierge's job**, and the unchecked badge is what makes that visible rather than aspirational.

> **⚠⚠ Photograph the maker's mark — it is read.** A close-up taken with the *Detail of last* toggle is sent to the agent *with the object it belongs to*, in the same look, which is the entire reason those shots exist. Flip the china over, zoom in on the signature, photograph the hallmark, and the name that comes back is a reading rather than a guess. A detail shot is still never a line of its own and is never numbered.

> **⚠⚠ Two photographs of one object: the close-up is handled, a second Items shot is NOT.** Anthony asked the question outright — *"we take two pictures of that bar … it's not going to think it's more of the same items is it"* — and the honest answer is that it depends entirely on which button was used. **Every photograph is its own separate look and the agent carries no memory between them**, so it cannot know it has seen the credenza already.

| What you shoot | What comes back |
| --- | --- |
| The cabinet, then **Detail of last** on the maker's mark | **One line.** The close-up rides in the same look as its parent and is never a line of its own. This is the case the toggle exists for |
| The cabinet, then the maker's mark as an ordinary Items shot | **Two lines.** Nothing knows they are the same plate |
| The credenza shot twice on the Items pass | **Double.** Four objects in the frame come back as eight lines |

> **So the field rule is one Items shot per object or shelf, and *Detail of last* for every second angle.** The toggle resets after each shot, so it is one deliberate tap per close-up.

> **⚠ And the desk catches what the field missed: *Possible duplicates*.** Any two agent-named lines in the same room carrying the same name, **off different photographs**, are flagged in a block above the rows with both thumbnails side by side, and each row wears a bronze *possible duplicate* chip. Two answers, and the block will not choose for you:

| Button | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| **Remove** | Takes that line off the inventory. **The photograph is untouched in Google Drive** — only the line goes — and *Removed items* at the foot of the tab gives it back whole |
| **Not duplicates** | They really are two objects. Clears the pair and stops flagging them |

> **It flags and never merges, and that is not caution — the app cannot know.** Two rows reading *Nightstand* in one bedroom are either a duplicated frame or a matching pair, and only somebody who can see both photographs can say which. Merging automatically would silently delete a real object off a schedule a court may read. **Lines split off ONE frame are never flagged** — two matching lamps in one photograph are two real objects the agent saw at once, and the row already says *1 of 2 in this photo*. Correcting either name, or ticking the row reviewed, settles it on its own.

> **Two things it raises that are not about naming at all.** Both render above the rows, and neither is ticked or acted on by the app. **Must find** — it reads the intake's must-find answers (the ones not yet ticked found) against each photograph it is sent to name: the unnamed Items-pass shots in that run, with their detail close-ups. It says when it sees the item or somewhere one would be kept (a safe, a strongbox, a locked drawer). Nothing else in the app reads those answers against the photographs. **⚠ It never sees the as-found shots**, and a shot named before the run is not sent again, so a safe that shows only in an as-found wide shot is not caught: read the as-found set against the must-find list yourself. (Corrected 2026-09-30: this said it checked every frame in the house, the as-found wide shots included. It never has, and it will not: Anthony decided on 2026-09-30 that Agent One looks only at the detailed inventory photographs.) **Firearms and possible NFA items** — a suppressor reads as a plain metal tube, and never being categorised as a firearm at all is the real risk the protocol names. It sets the *Firearms* category and raises a notice; it never sets the NFA flag. **Read off the photographs, not off the house** — a notice is a prompt to go and look, never a clearance.

> **A frame with nothing in it is a real answer.** A wall, an empty room, a misfire comes back with no name and no line invented for it — but it is stamped, so the desk can tell it from a photograph the agent never reached. It stays on the *Unnamed shots* count for a person to deal with.

> **⚠⚠ It needs an Apps Script redeploy and an API key.** The naming runs server-side and not in the app, because `havellin.html` is a public file and a key in it is not a key. So it needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in **Project Settings → Script Properties** and a deployment of `main-sync.gs` at **2026-09-22b** or later, and the 2026-09-30 deployment (§2) for its full answer limit: on an older one a busy photograph can fail as *the answer was cut off before it finished*. Run `testAgentIdentify()` from the Apps Script editor first — it takes no arguments, sends one real photograph and prints what came back, so a setup problem can never be mistaken for a naming problem. Until both are done the button reports the reason rather than doing nothing. Cost is roughly **$6 an estate**.

### The item record runs on every labour job — and only some of it is estate work (new 2026-09-21)

Anthony: *“we should have the room by room inventory available on all job types … what’s going to the new home, what’s going to be donated, what’s going to be sent to the kids, what’s going to be auctioned … the inventory is not just for the jobs that we’re doing for dead people.”* There are **two separate questions** here and the app now answers them separately.

| Axis | Question | Which jobs | What it costs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1 · the item record** | Does this job keep a room-by-room record of what is here and where it went? | **All six labour services.** Home Editing, Home Transition, Home Cleanout, Estate Settlement, Probate, Contested Probate | **Nothing.** No estimate moves by a dollar. There is no *document* step on a living job and no documentation-scope control |
| **2 · the fiduciary apparatus** | Must this record satisfy a court and a personal representative? | **Decedent jobs only** — Estate Settlement, Probate, Contested Probate | This is what the *document* step prices (§5c) |

> **⚠ The capture was never gated; the DOCUMENTS were, and in the wrong direction.** The room grid, the camera, the chips and the manifest have rendered on every labour service since they were built — so half the premise above was already true. What was not true is that the seven estate documents were withheld. Measured on a real living Home Editing client: the **Release Approval Request** printed *“To Personal Representative”* over *“Fair Market Value as of date of death (—)”*, the Estate Inventory Report was headed *Estate Inventory* and flagged *not stated* in red on every line, and the Court Inventory offered a §733.604 schedule with an adoption block to sign. One *Donate* row was all it took.

**What a living-client job produces instead: the Contents Record.** One button, on the Job Admin & Inv tab, where the estate documents sit on a decedent job. It is grouped by destination and then by room, and it answers the family’s question rather than the court’s: *what stayed, what went, where each piece went and who took it*. It carries **no FMV, no valuation basis and no appraisal list** — Anthony ruled valuation out of these jobs (*“not the pricing agent”*) — but it does carry **gross, fees and net on any line where a figure was actually received** from a consignment or an auction, because that is a recorded actual and the first thing a family asks about the dining table.

> **It is the mid-job control as well as the hand-over.** Print it on a Tuesday to see what is still undecided; the family gets the same page at close-out. It stamps *IN PROGRESS* until every line has been reviewed and *REVIEWED* after, so an early copy cannot be mistaken for a final one. Client documents are never withheld while a job is running — that is a standing rule, not an oversight.

**“Where it went” is a destination and a person, and both are on the row.** The disposition list gained **Distribute** and **Move**; the *Channel / Recipient* column is promoted onto the row itself rather than living three sections deep in the item panel.

| Disposition | Means | Is it a release? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Move** | Goes to the client’s new home. The destination is the job’s own recorded new address, so there is nobody to name | **No.** It is still the client’s property, in the client’s house |
| **Distribute** | A named person takes it — a daughter, a grandson, a friend | **Yes.** Put the name in *Channel / Recipient* |

> **⚠ It is *Distribute* and not *Gift*, on purpose, and do not rename it.** On an estate a bequest handed to a beneficiary is a **distribution under the will**, not a gift — the two differ for basis and for whether the event belongs on a Form 709 or a 706. One word has to be right on both sides of the catalogue and *Distribute* is the one that is.

> **⚠⚠ Adding it closed three live defects on ESTATE work, which is the part worth knowing.** (1) There was no disposition meaning *given to a named person*, so a specific bequest released to its beneficiary reached neither the awaiting-approval list nor the Release Approval Request — the one document that is supposed to catch it. (2) *Channel / Recipient* was the only field recording who got something and it was **not on the sticky-field list** while all four of its neighbours were, so a two-device merge kept *when* the sideboard left and lost *who took it*. (3) A firearm marked for a named person read as **CLEARED TO CARRY**, because the transport gate’s *dealer named* test is satisfied by any non-empty recipient. A beneficiary is not a licensed dealer; it is blocked outright now, above the authority test.

> **⚠⚠ The client’s Drive workbook stopped asserting a death (new 2026-09-21; in `saveInventory.gs` since Apps Script 2026-09-21a, §2).** The on-screen summary was gated when the item record went to all six services; the **workbook** — the spreadsheet that lands in the client’s own Drive folder, which is the copy they keep — was not. A Home Editing client’s own file opened with *Date of Death*, *Letters Issued* and *§733.604 Inventory Deadline* over three empty cells, counted *Items Awaiting Valuation* on an engagement that values nothing, and flagged *Exempt §732.402* against a statute that needs a decedent. On a living job those rows are gone; the flag survives as *Promised to someone*, the category block counts rather than prices, the net reads *Net received*, and the footer says what the record is not instead of naming a court filing. **The file is still called *Estate Inventory — HVL-xxxx* and that is deliberate** — Anthony: *“could be a fancy name for a big house. not always a dead person’s estate”* — and the workbook is looked up by that name, so renaming it would create a second file rather than rename the one counsel already has a link to.

> **⚠⚠ And it stopped asserting a COURT on a trust matter (new 2026-09-21; since Apps Script 2026-09-21b, §2).** Same sheet, one level in. A trust estate is still an estate, so it kept the full estate layout — including *Letters Issued*, a *§733.604 Inventory Deadline* and an *Exempt §732.402* count, all three of which are creatures of a probate administration. Letters are issued by a probate court appointing a personal representative, §733.604 is a filing deadline in that proceeding, and the §732.402 allowance is *petitioned for* in it. A successor trustee’s own spreadsheet opened with a statutory court deadline over an empty cell. Those three rows now come off on a matter recorded **Trust** or **Neither**, and the footer names what is administered under the trust instrument instead of citing a court filing. **Everything else stays** — and on an estate that is most of the sheet.

> **⚠⚠ What deliberately does NOT come off a trust matter, and do not “finish the job” by taking it.** **Date of Death, the valuation basis, Total Estimated FMV, Items Awaiting Valuation and the MAIV aggregate all stay** (⚠ the first four now only where the tier says we state values at all — see the note below, which is a different question from this one), because none of them is a probate thing: at death tangible personal property is valued at date-of-death FMV, and that one number is the §1014 stepped-up basis, the Form 706 figure and the carrying value a §736.08135 trust accounting opens with — and the §1014 step-up reaches revocable trust assets, so a successor trustee needs exactly the number a personal representative does. ***Specific Bequests* stays too**: a will’s specific gift is directed by the instrument whether or not anything probates, and the column it counts is headed *Specific Bequest*, so renaming the rollup would make the Summary disagree with the sheet beside it. **A pour-over will (*Both*) keeps everything** — it really does have a probate estate beside the trust — and so does an **unanswered** matter type, which is every job recorded before 2026-09-21. Guessing the other way would strip a live court deadline off a real probate matter on the strength of a question nobody was asked.

> **⚠⚠ AND IT STOPPED ASSERTING A VALUATION THE ENGAGEMENT WAS CONTRACTED NOT TO PRODUCE (new 2026-09-22; since Apps Script 2026-09-22a, §2).** The same sheet, a third time, on a third axis. The first two ask *is there a decedent* and *is there a court*; this one asks **what the client is contracted to receive** (§4). At the *Contents list* tier the agreement says counsel builds the inventory and Havellin *“states no opinion of value”*, and at *None* counsel does the whole thing — and both surfaces still opened with the full valuation apparatus. Measured on a probate estate with two unvalued items: the **screen** carried a *Valuation Basis* dropdown, a *Value as of* line with a §2032 alternate-valuation tick, *Total Estimated FMV $0*, *Items Awaiting Valuation 2* and an *FMV by Category* table priced at $0 on every row; the **workbook** carried the FMV total, the awaiting count and the FMV rollup. ***Items Awaiting Valuation* is the one that can never correct itself** — at this tier nobody enters a value *by contract*, so the count equals the item count on every write and never falls, which is exactly how a reader learns to skip the whole block. Both surfaces now count instead of pricing, and the two valuation controls come off with them.

> **⚠ What stays at the contents tier, and the MAIV row is the one worth arguing about.** Everything that is a fact about the *matter* rather than a claim about *our deliverable*: Date of Death, Letters, the §733.604 deadline, the §732.402 count, Specific Bequests, Disputed / Hold, Gross / Fees / Net, the item count and the footer. **So does the MAIV aggregate**, and deliberately: Treas. Reg. §20.2031-6(b) puts an *expert appraisal under oath with the Form 706* on the **estate**, and that requirement does not stop applying because somebody else is doing the valuing. At this tier every article is unvalued, so the notice reads *“the $3,000 aggregate cannot be tested yet … value them before the return is prepared”* — which is precisely what counsel needs to see. Withholding it would suppress a federal filing prompt on an estate that owes one. **The seven valuation COLUMNS on the item sheet stay too**: a blank column is not a claim, a headline total is.

> **⚠ An estate with no tier recorded keeps the valuation, and nothing priced before 2026-09-22 moves.** An unanswered tier reads as *Inventory with values* (§4), so every estate on the books behaves exactly as it did. What changes it is answering the question — and since 2026-09-22 Build Estimate will not commit a price until you have. **Fixing the workbook alone would have recreated the defect the living-client build closed**, which is why the screen moved in the same change: the whole finding there was that the screen and the spreadsheet must not disagree. Doing it also closed a disagreement that was *already live* — the workbook has counted rather than priced on a living job since 2026-09-21 and the screen beside it still showed an *FMV by Category* table of $0 rows.

### What this tab is for — rebuilt 2026-09-01

The Job Plan is the **field** surface: room by room, on a phone, standing in the room. The Job Admin & Inv tab is the **desk** surface — that evening, the concierge opens the day's captures and works them: puts values on things, decides where each goes, ticks each line off, then produces the client's schedule. It is not a field tab (it is hidden in field mode) and that is deliberate.

- **Grouped by disposition, then by room.** *Not yet decided* sorts first, because it is the worklist; the rest run Keep · Move · Distribute · Auction · Consign · Sell · Donate · Junk · Hold — the order the decisions get made in, not alphabetical. Inside each group the rooms run in **walkthrough order**, so the evening review retraces the day.
- **Needs you** — a row of counts across the top: *Unnamed shots · Not yet decided · No value yet · Lots to split · Needs appraiser · No recipient · No signed receipt · Firearms held · Disputed / Hold*. *No value yet*, *Lots to split* and *No signed receipt* (new 2026-10-03: released to a person with no signed receipt filed) are estate-only, and *Lots to split* shows only on an estate that has not answered **No** to the Form 706 question; on a living-client job *Needs appraiser* reads *To a specialist*. *No recipient* counts what is leaving with nobody named in *Channel / Recipient*. Each is also a filter. These are the reasons to open the tab.
- **Today / Last 7 days / All**, plus a room picker and a search box. Each client's desk opens clean: on **Today** if anything was captured there today, so the evening starts on the day's work, and on **All** if not, so a job picked up a week later is not an empty screen; no search, no count filter and no room. The default is applied once per client opened, and what you choose then stays until you open another client. Both since 2026-09-30: pressing **All** used to snap back to Today on every repaint on a day with a capture, and the filters used to follow you from one client to the next (a room by its position, so into a different room of a different house, and a Today onto a client with nothing shot that day).
- **The name, the value and the disposition edit on the row**, and so does *Channel / Recipient* on anything leaving or moving. The ▾ opens the item panel — the photo, and every other field in four sections (Item · Valuation · Disposition · Flags & Track). On a firearm it also carries a **Firearm** block: whether the firearm may be carried and, on one going to a named person, its **dealer route** (since 2026-09-30; see *The transport gate*). Every line's panel has **Remove line** (below).
- **Select rows and a bulk bar rises from the foot:** set category, disposition, valuation source (on an estate), room or recipient across the whole selection, mark them reviewed, raise an approval request, or record a signed one. The evening pass is mostly the same decision repeated forty times; doing it one dropdown at a time is what makes people stop doing it. *Set valuation source…* is offered only where the item panel shows that field, which is an estate; until 2026-09-30 a living job's bar offered it too and wrote a value nobody could see, and the bar refuses it there now.
- **Remove line** (new 2026-09-30), in each line's panel, takes one line off the inventory — photographed, split out of a photograph, or typed. It asks first, naming the line, and it **never deletes the photograph**: another line may share it, and the room's 🗑 is the only thing that deletes an image. The removal is recorded (when, and the concierge assigned to the job) and the number is never given to anything else. Until this date only a typed line (*Remove line item*) or an agent's possible duplicate could come off the desk. *Removed items* at the foot of the tab lists it and **Restore** gives it back, photograph and all.
- **Reviewed** is a tick per line with an *N of M* read-out on the tab and on each group.
- The Summary and its rollups moved to the **bottom** and start closed — *More → Show summary & rollups*. It is a read-out, not a workspace, and it was sitting on top of the work.

> **The column-group buttons are gone, and so is the table they existed for.** Until 2026-09-01 this tab drew all 29 manifest columns and put a row of buttons above them (Valuation · Disposition · Flags & Track · Everything) to swap which slice was on screen. A control that exists only to work around the width of the thing beneath it is a symptom, not a feature. The column *groups* survive — they are now the item panel's four sections, so there is still exactly one definition of what a column is and where it belongs. The **export is unchanged**: the workbook and the CSV carry every column.

> **Reviewing gates nothing and locks nothing, on purpose.** Havellin shows clients work in progress during an engagement, so a checkpoint that withholds the document is exactly the wrong mechanism. An unfinished review instead **stamps every client document** — *IN PROGRESS — 18 of 42 items reviewed*, with a line saying unreviewed values may still change. A finished one reads *REVIEWED*. Do not turn this into a gate later; the honesty comes from the stamp.

> **⚠⚠ One photograph can carry several inventory lines (new 2026-09-20).** Anthony, off a photograph of a bar console: *"this same picture is going to serve as reference to multiple items — four bottles of booze, a Banksy, a hutch — so we really don't have to photograph each individual item unless let's say we know it's extremely valuable and we want to take a picture of the front and the back, or this Banksy if it was real we would zoom in on the signature of the artist, or if it's China we'd flip it over and get the maker's mark."* That is how the work is done, and until now the manifest could not express it: one shot was one line, so five objects in one frame meant either five photographs of one shelf or four objects going unrecorded. Open the line and press **➕ Another item in this photo**; a new line appears sharing the image, and you name, categorise, value and disposition it on its own. Each one gets its own permanent item number, because a receipt cites each object separately. The row says *1 of 4 in this photo* so two rows under identical thumbnails are not read as a duplicated import. **Since 2026-09-30 the new line is drawn in place** in *Not yet decided*, beside the photograph's other lines where they are undecided too, and only what it changes is repainted: those other lines, the group and room counts, and the counts at the top. It used to rebuild the whole list: 1.4 seconds on a 3,000-line estate when first measured, and about 2.5 on a busy machine, against under a tenth of a second now.

> **It is a LINE and not a photograph, and that distinction is load-bearing.** The Job Plan room card counts *shots taken* — a console split into four objects still reads *1 items* there, because the crew is checking whether they shot the room and not how many things were in the frame. It is a full line everywhere a LINE is counted: the manifest, the worklist counts, the Court Inventory, the Estate Inventory PDF, the CSV and the workbook. **⚠ It is not the same thing as a detail shot.** A *detail* is a second photograph of ONE object (the maker's mark, the signature) and is never a line at all; this is the converse, one photograph and several objects. Splitting a line that was itself split rebases onto the photograph rather than chaining, so removing one cannot orphan the rest.

> **⚠ Two refusals, and both are the honest answer.** A shot that has **not reached Drive** cannot be split: the new line would carry no image and never get one, and would print on the attorney's schedule with an empty photo box for the rest of the engagement. Press Retry on the shot first. And **discarding the photograph takes every line split out of it** — they exist only because the image does — so the confirmation *names* them (*#13 Banksy print · #14 B&O speaker*) rather than counting them, and *Removed items* on this tab gives each line back with its own Restore, but not the image: that is in Google Drive's bin for 30 days (the row says *photo in the Drive bin*) and is restored there. Nothing is copied from the source line but the image: no name, no category, no value, no disposition, no flags, because it is a different object and a prefilled answer on a blank line is how something gets filed by silence.

### Categories & appraiser routing

Every item has a category from the shared taxonomy. Each category knows which specialist appraiser it routes to and whether items in it are treated as carrying **marked artistic or intrinsic value** — which drives two *different* $3,000 tests, one per item and one across the estate. See *Two different $3,000 tests* below; confusing them is the mistake this section exists to prevent. Categories: Antiques · Art & Décor · Collectibles · Electronics & Appliances · Firearms · Furniture · General/Household · Jewelry & Watches · Musical Instruments · Rugs & Carpets · Silver & Precious Metal · Vehicles & Watercraft · Wine & Spirits.

### From the estimate walkthrough (the bridge)

When flagged collections or vehicles exist on the estimate, a **From the Estimate Walkthrough** panel offers to add them to the manifest. Collections can be **itemized** (one row per piece) or kept as a **lot**; a guessed category pre-selects the appraiser routing. Collector vehicles carry a needs-appraisal flag. Imported items can't be added twice. Until they are brought in, the Job Plan says so in a banner and the Appraisal Worklist in a note, and both send you here, to the **Job Admin & Inv** tab (since 2026-09-30: the banner named a tab called *Inventory*, and the worklist said *add them on the Job Plan*, which has no import, and counted every flagged line on the estimate whether or not it had been brought in).

> **⚠⚠ A collection you marked *Appraise* on Build Estimate used to lose that instruction on the way in — fixed 2026-09-11.** It arrived as *Hold*, which is where disputed property goes and which sorts last, and it carried no appraisal flag at all — so it only ever reached the worklist if the category guesser happened to land on an intrinsic category. Measured: *"Asian ceramics"* matches none of the guesser's keywords, so it became General/Household, which is not intrinsic, and **an explicit written instruction to appraise was reported on no document anywhere.** Same for "grandfather clock", "porcelain" and "doll collection". It now arrives as *Not yet decided* — the honest answer, since appraising first is exactly why the destination is open — with the appraisal flag set. **Any collection imported before 2026-09-11 is worth checking against the estimate**; tick *Needs Appraisal* on the row if the estimate said Appraise.

### Appraisers (per estate)

Add a roster of named appraisers — name, firm, **credential** (ISA / ASA / AAA / USPAP / GIA), **independence**, effective (value) / report dates, and **Due back**. Pick one from the Vendor Directory, or type one in for a one-off. A firm the directory also lists as a buyer, a dealer, a reseller, an auction house or an estate sale company is marked in the picker, and *Independent* is unticked for you: flagged, never refused (§13). Link an item to a roster appraiser from the *Appraisal Doc* field in its item panel, or from its row on the guardrail panel — replacing free-text with a defensible record. Linking fills *Valuation Source* with *Appraisal* when it is blank (never over a source somebody chose), and unlinking clears an *Appraisal* source.

> **Due back is the "expected turnaround" the deliverables page promises** ("which appraiser is engaged for each, and expected turnaround"). Nothing in the app recorded it until 2026-09-01. It prints on the Appraisal Flag List at the foot of the Estate Inventory Report; with no due date set, the report falls back to the report date and then to a dash.

### The $3,000 appraisal guardrail

Items flagged for appraisal that aren't yet linked to an appraiser surface a panel: a **soft amber nudge on Standard** jobs, a **red block on Formal** jobs (the inventory isn't complete until each is appraised). Per-item **Waive** logs a reason. On a Formal job, the Court Inventory export stamps *DRAFT* until every flagged item is appraised or waived.

**Two ways an item gets onto that panel, and they are different questions.** *Automatically*, on an estate, from its category: an intrinsic category (art, antiques, jewelry, silver, rugs, collectibles, firearms, wine, instruments) with no value yet, or with a value at or above the threshold ($3,000, or $500 on a disputed estate). A line in any other category never routes on its value, and on a living-client job nothing routes automatically; only the tick does. Or *because somebody said so*: tick **Needs Appraisal** in the item panel's Valuation section (new 2026-09-11), which is the desk half of the field toggle in §10. Use it for anything the category cannot see, and untick it to take a line back off the worklist without recording a waiver.

> **Waive and untick are not the same act.** *Waive* records that the requirement applied and a named person decided against it, with a reason, and that reason prints. Unticking *Needs Appraisal* just says the flag should not have been set. If the item routes automatically on its category, unticking does nothing — waive it or link an appraiser.

### Two different $3,000 tests — per item, and across the estate

The app carries two thresholds that share a number and answer different questions. Read this once and the rest of the section follows.

| Test | Question | Where it shows |
|---|---|---|
| **Per item** — $3,000, or $500 on an estate with a recorded dispute or on Contested Probate | Is *this object* worth enough that a specialist should value it? | The ⚑ flag on the row, the guardrail panel, the per-specialist groups on the Appraisal Worklist |
| **Aggregate** — Treas. Reg. §20.2031-6(b), $3,000, never indexed | Do the estate's articles of marked artistic or intrinsic value, *added up*, exceed $3,000 — so that an expert's appraisal under oath must be filed with the Form 706? | The *MAIV Articles* row in the Summary, and its own block on the Appraisal Worklist |
| **Grouping** — Treas. Reg. §20.2031-6(a), $100 an article, never indexed | May *this lot* stand as a group on the 706 schedule, or must its articles be named one by one? | A live readout in *From the Estimate Walkthrough* as the lot is created, the **Lots to split** chip on this tab, and its own block on the Appraisal Worklist |

> **An estate can pass every per-item test and still fail the aggregate.** Thirty $500 pieces of silver: not one is near $3,000, every one carries a recorded value, so nothing is flagged and the Appraisal Worklist has *no groups on it at all* — and the estate owes an expert appraisal under oath on $15,000 of silverware. Until 2026-08-24 the app said nothing in that case. The aggregate block now prints whether or not anything made the per-item cut, and the empty-worklist message points at it.


### The grouping cap — Treas. Reg. §20.2031-6(a)

A **lot** is any manifest line whose quantity is more than one: forty pieces of kitchen sundries under a single description and a single value. The regulation allows that on a federal return only where no article inside the group is worth more than **$100**. Above it, each article is named specifically.

| What the app sees | What it says |
| --- | --- |
| 6 articles, $5,000 — averages $833 | **Above the cap.** Itemize it, or record on the line why it is properly grouped. |
| 40 articles, $800 — averages $20 | Inside the cap. Stated rather than left silent, so you can tell a checked lot from an unchecked one. |
| 25 articles, no value recorded | **Cannot be tested.** Never reported as clear — an untested lot is not a lot inside the cap. |
| 1 article, $50,000 | Nothing. A single article is already named specifically; the cap is about grouping. |

> **⚠⚠ IT IS TESTED WHEN THE LOT IS CREATED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON IT WORKS.** A lot row carries **one** name, **one** value and **one** quantity — there is no per-article data inside it for any later rule to split. That is exactly why the retired threshold could never have been implemented as a rendering rule, and why the readout sits in *From the Estimate Walkthrough*, where the *Keep as lot / Itemize* selector is one tap from the number. Change the quantity or the mode and the line under it re-reads live.

> **⚠ The per-article figure is an average, and every surface says so.** FMV on a manifest line is the *line total*, so the article value a lot implies is that total over its count. Thirty forks and one candlestick can average under the cap with the candlestick over it. It is the only figure a grouped line can support — and an average *above* the cap is a certainty that something inside is above it, which is the direction that matters.

> **⚠ It flags, it never refuses.** At the moment a collection is imported, its value is a walkthrough estimate and its count is often a guess, so blocking would refuse on a figure nobody has stood behind yet. The panel names the arithmetic and names the fix; the **Lots to split** chip and the Appraisal Worklist catch whatever is committed anyway, including lots typed by hand or edited later.

> **⚠ The documentation level does not move this — the 706 answer does.** A recorded dispute forces Strict Mode without making a federal return due, so reading the level here would assert a federal itemisation standard on an estate that files nothing. The gate is the same one the §20.2031-6(b) aggregate reads: one regulation, two subsections, one gate, both blocks on one page of the Appraisal Worklist. On an estate answering **No** to the 706 question there is no chip, no readout and no block. **Unanswered still counts as yes**, as it does everywhere else, so the cap applies by default on every new estate.

> **⚠ Not verified against a primary source.** The exact wording of §20.2031-6(a) was read from secondary summaries — the egress proxy blocks ecfr.gov, law.cornell.edu and law.justia.com from the build environment, which §8 of the estate scope spec already records for the §736.08135 mechanics. The *$100 an article* reading is what the app implements. It rides the counsel review bundle with the agreement language; confirm the article-versus-lot-total reading before a real 706 estate relies on it.

### MAIV — marked artistic or intrinsic value

Two manifest columns: **MAIV §20.2031-6** (Auto / Yes / No) and **MAIV Class**.

- **It fires by default** on Art & Décor, Antiques, Jewelry & Watches, Silver & Precious Metal, Rugs & Carpets, Collectibles, Firearms, Wine & Spirits and Musical Instruments. Furniture, Electronics, General/Household and Vehicles do not.
- **The control overrides in both directions**, and both directions matter. A $40 mass-produced print sitting in Art & Décor is not an article of marked artistic value and carrying it in overstates the estate's exposure — set it to *No*. A fur coat or a rare book library filed under General/Household *is* one and nothing derives it — set it to *Yes* and pick the class.
- **MAIV Class uses the regulation's own words** — paintings/prints/statuary · antiques · jewelry · **furs** · silverware · **books & manuscripts** · oriental rugs · coin & stamp collections · other articles of marked value. Furs and books have no item category on purpose: the class is a separate field precisely so they can be captured without inventing a fourteenth category.
- **The class cell only opens on an article that is MAIV**, the same rule the NFA tick follows on non-firearms.

> **Counted across the whole gross estate, not the probate schedule.** The regulation says "included in the gross estate", and a revocable trust's contents are in the gross estate even though they sit outside the §733.604 filing. So Trust and Non-probate items *are* in the aggregate — which matters here, where a great many estates hold everything in trust. Do not reuse the Court Inventory's probate filter for this.

> **An untested aggregate is never reported as one under the cap.** An article with no value recorded makes the total a *floor*. While anything is unvalued the app says "at least $X … cannot be tested yet" and refuses to conclude, because printing "under the aggregate" against a partial sum reads as a clearance and is not one.

> **The filing requirement is scoped to the 706, not to Strict Mode.** A recorded dispute forces Strict Mode without making a federal return due, so the block reads the *706 answer itself*. On an estate filing no return the aggregate still prints — it is a useful read on where the value sits — but as a guide, never as a requirement.

### Valuation basis & date

- **Valuation Basis** — the level of value the whole schedule is stated on (defaults to Fair Market Value, as estate/probate requires).
- **Value as of** — normally the date of death. Toggle the **§2032 alternate valuation date** (six months after death, for taxable estates) and item value-dates and the Court Inventory follow it.
- **Asset Track** — Probate / Trust / Non-probate / Homestead / Exempt. **The default follows the matter type**: *Trust* on a matter recorded *Trust*, *Probate* on everything else including *Both*, *Neither* and an unanswered one. Probate-track and Exempt-track items appear on the **Court Inventory**; Trust-track items appear on the **Trust Schedule**. **⚠ The two lists are NOT complementary** — Non-probate and Homestead property is on neither, deliberately, because each passes outside both and is reported elsewhere, and each document names what it left out. **⚠ The instruction to set the track by hand on a trust matter is withdrawn (2026-09-21).** A track you set still always wins.
- **Valuation Basis / Comps** (new 2026-09-01) — free text beside the value: which valuation app you used, which comparables, what the range was. The concierge reads a value off an external app that returns recent auction results; this is where that answer is written down.
- **Serial Number** (new 2026-09-13) — the object's own identifier, as the world writes it. *Item #* is ours and means nothing outside this app; a dealer's receipt, an insurance claim and a police report are all written **serial by serial**, and until this was added none of them could be reconciled against the manifest. It is **not firearms-only** — a vehicle has a VIN, a watch and an instrument have serials — so the field is offered on every row and simply left blank where it does not apply. On a firearm it is a *gate*: see the transport rule in the firearms section below.

> **A value with no stated source is what the published promise exists to prevent.** The deliverables page commits to "date-of-death fair market value, *and the valuation source stated*". The Estate Inventory Report prints **not stated** in red against any line whose Valuation Source is blank, rather than leaving a gap the reader has to notice for themselves.

### Chain of custody & snapshots

- **🔗 custody log** (per item) — record each transfer: released / received / transferred / returned · party · date · method · receipt. A badge shows the event count.
- **Snapshot** — capture a labeled point-in-time copy of the manifest (the amended-inventory / what-changed trail); print any snapshot as an as-of schedule, headed *Inventory Snapshot* since 2026-10-03 (it said *Estate Inventory*, which a trust is not). **A snapshot is never deleted (since 2026-10-03, P19).** Each row in the list offers **Void**, which asks why and keeps the snapshot listed, marked *VOIDED* with the reason, who and when, and still printable, its print carrying the same block. Until then *Remove* took one confirm and the snapshot was gone from every device, and Cancel on the label question took an unlabelled snapshot anyway; it takes none now. **Compare with now** and **Compare with previous** print *Inventory Snapshot — What Changed*: the snapshot against the inventory as it stands, or the last snapshot before it that is not voided against this one, matched by item number, which is never reissued — each line added, removed, renamed, revalued, moved to another disposition or another track, counted and listed, with both totals. A line in a snapshot taken before item numbers existed is counted apart, never matched by name. *Compare with previous* is offered only where there is an earlier snapshot to compare with.

> **⚠⚠ Until 2026-09-11 an unrelated edit to any field on the row destroyed the custody log.** The manifest merges item by item, and within an item it took the whole *newer record* — right for a value somebody corrected, and wrong for an append-only log. Measured on the real merge: Ashley records *Released · Sotheby's · receipt SBY-4471* on the laptop, Anthony corrects the same item's value on the iPad without having synced, his record is newer, and the log comes back **empty**. Two people each logging one event kept one of them. **Any custody log on a job worked from two devices before 2026-09-11 is worth re-reading** — events may be missing and nothing said so.

> **Custody events are now UNIONED across devices rather than won.** Each event carries its own identity from the moment it is recorded, so two people's entries both survive and two genuine handovers to the same party on the same day stay two events. What you see is ordered by *when the event happened*, not by which device synced last.

> **Removing an event marks it removed; it does not erase it.** That is what makes the union safe — a spliced event is simply re-added by the next sync from a device that still has it. The removal is what survives the merge, in both directions, so a device that still holds the event live cannot quietly undo it. The event count beside the link, and the list inside it, both ignore removed events.

> **⚠ The union is on the server too, from Apps Script `2026-09-11a`.** `saveInventory.gs` carries the same union — it is the durable store, so fixing only the app would leave an event alive on two devices and dead in the sheet. `main-sync.gs` changed in the same deploy: the two dead `Estimates` / `Hours` writers were removed and `BACKEND_VERSION` bumped to `2026-09-11a`. They are one project and one deployment, and every later deployment carries both; what is owed today is in §2.

### Close-out records: the Disposition Ledger, proceeds statements and donations (new 2026-10-03, P19)

Anthony, 2026-10-03, on the estate workflow: *“build it all”*. The agreement and the client estimate ask the client to sign off the final disposition summary, and until this date nothing was one; *proceeds reconciled* was a tick box nothing checked; and *donation receipts* passed on one receipt for any number of donated lines. Under the inventory, above the snapshots, the desk now carries up to three cards, on every job that keeps an item record (Home Prep has none), once the inventory has a line:

- **Disposition Ledger** — *the close-out summary the client signs.* The card reads how many lines are on the ledger, the gross, the fees and the net to whoever holds the proceeds, names what is missing, and carries **Print**, **File to Drive** (*Update the filed copy* once it is filed, with the filed copy linked) and **File signed copy**, which files the signed page to the client’s *Signed Records* folder; the signed copies are listed under it. It left *More ▾*, where it was offered on estates only: a living client’s ledger is net to the client, and the client signs it.
- **Proceeds statements** (Home Cleanout and the estate services, the jobs that sell) — **+ Record a statement** takes an auction house’s, consignment shop’s or estate sale company’s statement, or a buyer’s bill of sale: who issued it, its date, the lines it covers (ticked from the lines marked Auction, Consign or Sell), the gross, the commission and fees, the net paid, when and to which account, and its reference. It is refused, naming everything missing, without who, the date, a line, the gross or the net paid, and a negative figure is refused. Each statement is checked against the ledger to the cent: its gross and fees against what its lines record, and its net paid against gross less fees. A difference of a cent is flagged on its row with both figures — *Gross: $1,250 on the statement, $1,237.50 on its lines ($12.50 apart)* — and so is a line no longer on the inventory, a line on two statements, and a sold line on none. The statement itself is filed from its row (**File the statement**, to *Signed Records*). It is never deleted: **Void** asks why and keeps it listed, voided, with the reason.
- **Donations** — one row per charity, as named in *Channel / Recipient* (case and spaces aside), with how many of its lines are receipted, **Print Donation Record** and **File charity receipt** (to *Signed Records*). Lines with no charity recorded are listed together, last, to be named first. A filed receipt covers the charity’s lines as they stood when it was filed; a line with a reference of its own in *Receipt / Doc* counts as receipted too.

> **⚠⚠ THE LEDGER IS A RECORD OF WHERE EACH ITEM WENT AND WHAT IT BROUGHT, AND IT IS SIGNED.** It was headed *Disposition & Accounting Ledger — Fiduciary accounting of disposed items · proceeds net to the estate*: an accounting it is not (the accounting is the representative’s, with counsel), a fiduciary on a living job, which has none, and an estate on a trust. It carried no item numbers, so no statement could be checked against it, had no sign-off, and was never filed. Since 2026-10-03 it is headed *Disposition Ledger — Where each item went and what it brought · proceeds net to* the estate, the trust, *the estate or the trust, as the property is held* on a pour-over will, or the client on living work, and its footer says the same. Every line with a disposition, a recipient, proceeds or a date is on it, under its **item number**: the item, the disposition, the channel or recipient, who authorised it and when (since 2026-10-05, each signer with the day they signed: *Ruth Adler (Oct 1, 2026); Daniel Adler (Oct 8, 2026)*), the date, gross, fees, net, and the receipt — a filed signed receipt, charity’s receipt or proceeds statement covering the line is linked, otherwise the reference typed on the line. The totals add the lines to the cent, and a sale with nothing received reads *pending*, never $0. **Its gaps are named above the table**, where they are read before anybody signs: lines with no disposition, items that left with no recipient, sales with nothing recorded, a statement that does not agree, a sold line on no statement. The statements follow the table, each beside the lines it covers. **The sign-off** reads *“Reviewed and approved as the final record of the disposition of the property listed above.”* with a signature and date line for each fiduciary — the representative and every co-representative recorded, or one line for the role the matter names where none is — and for the client on living work. **While any line has no disposition the sign-off is withheld**, and the page says why: the rule the valued schedules follow for an unvalued line. An inventory with nothing on it has no ledger. The wording is drafted, not reviewed, and goes to counsel.

> **⚠ IT IS FILED TO DRIVE AS THE JOB CLOSES.** **Close job** files the page to the client’s *Estate Inventory* folder (the job’s root on an older folder without one), under the undated name the estate package files it under, so a later filing replaces the earlier one in place and Drive holds one ledger.
